related to holonomic D-modules and study
their properties especially for exponentially twisted
ones. Let
X
be a complex manifold of dimension
N

and
{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{+}(\mathbb{C}_{X})
the full
subcategory of
{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{X})

consisting of objects
F\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{X})

such that
F\simeq\mathbb{C}_{\{t\geq 0\}}\overset{+}{\otimes}F
. If for a holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module

\mathcal{M}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize hol}}(\mathcal{D}_{X})
there exists an
object
F\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{+}(\mathbb{C}_{X})
of

{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{+}(\mathbb{C}_{X})
such that

Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\mathbb{C}^{\rm E}_{X}\overset{+}{\otimes}F,

(5.1)

the following micro-supports of

F
introduced in Tamarkin
[
67
]

are useful in the study of
\mathcal{M}
. Let
X_{\mathbb{R}}
be the
underlying real analytic
manifold of
X
and by the standard coordinate

(t;t^{*})
of
T^{*}\mathbb{R}
identify
(T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathbb{R}

with the subset
(T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}})\times\{t^{*}=1\}\subset(T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}})\times(T^{*}\mathbb{R})
of

T^{*}(X_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R})\simeq(T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}})\times(T^{*}\mathbb{R})
. Let

\iota_{\mathbb{R}}:(T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathbb{R}\hookrightarrow T^{*}(X_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R})

(5.2)

be the inclusion map.
For an object
F\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{+}(\mathbb{C}_{X})
taking

\widetilde{F}\in{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{X\times\mathbb{R}})
such that

\mathbf{Q}(\widetilde{F})=F
we set

{\rm SS}^{{\rm E}}(F):=\iota_{\mathbb{R}}^{-1}{\rm SS}(\widetilde{F})\ \subset\ (T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathbb{R}.

(5.3)

This definition does not depend on the
choice of
\widetilde{F}
(see
[
10
, Section 2.6]
).
We call
{\rm SS}^{{\rm E}}(F)

the enhanced micro-support of
F
.
For a local coordinate
z=x+iy=(z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{N})
of
X

let
(x,y,t;x^{*},y^{*},t^{*})
be the
corresponding coordinate of
T^{*}(X_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R})\simeq(T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}})\times(T^{*}\mathbb{R})
.
Then on the open subset
\{t^{*}>0\}\subset T^{*}(X_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R})
locally we define a
real analytic map

\gamma:\{t^{*}>0\}\longrightarrow(T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathbb{R}
by

(x,y,t;x^{*},y^{*},t^{*})\longmapsto\Bigl(\bigl(x,y;\frac{x^{*}}{t^{*}},\frac{y^{*}}{t^{*}}\bigr),t\Bigr).

(5.4)

By the conicness of
{\rm SS}(\widetilde{F})

we can easily see that

{\rm SS}^{{\rm E}}(F)=\gamma\bigl({\rm SS}(\widetilde{F})\cap\{t^{*}>0\}\bigr).

(5.5)

Let
{\rm pr}_{T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}}}:(T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}}
be the projection and set

{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}(F):=\overline{{\rm pr}_{T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}}}\bigl({\rm SS}^{{\rm E}}(F)\bigr)}\ \subset\ T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}}

(5.6)

(see
[
10
, Section 2.6]
for a
different notation for it).
We call it the irregular micro-support of
F
.

From now on, we assume that
X
is a smooth algebraic
variety over
\mathbb{C}
and denote by
X^{{\rm an}}
its
underlying complex manifold that we sometimes
denote by
X
for short. We set
X_{\mathbb{R}}:=(X^{{\rm an}})_{\mathbb{R}}
. We shall consider
the micro-supports of the objects

F\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{+}(\mathbb{C}_{X^{{\rm an}}})
related to
the following special but basic
holonomic D-modules. The problem being local,
we may assume that
X
is affine. Then for a rational function

f=\frac{P}{Q}:X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
(
P,Q\in\Gamma(X;\mathcal{O}_{X})
,

Q\not=0
) on
X
we set

U:=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)
and
define an algebraic
exponential
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{E}^{f}_{U|X}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize hol}}(\mathcal{D}_{X})
as in the analytic case.
Let
i_{U}:U\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion map and

\mathcal{O}_{U}(f)\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize hol}}(\mathcal{D}_{U})
the algebraic
integrable connection on
U
associated to the
regular function
f:U\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
. Then
there exist isomorphisms

\mathcal{E}^{f}_{U|X}\simeq\mathbf{D}i_{U*}\mathcal{O}_{U}(f)\simeq(i_{U})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{U}(f).

(5.7)

We define the analytification

(\mathcal{E}^{f}_{U|X})^{{\rm an}}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize hol}}(\mathcal{D}_{X^{{\rm an}}})

of
\mathcal{E}^{f}_{U|X}
by

(\mathcal{E}^{f}_{U|X})^{{\rm an}}:=\mathcal{O}_{X^{{\rm an}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{E}^{f}_{U|X}

and set

Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{E}^{f}_{U|X}):=Sol_{X^{{\rm an}}}^{\rm E}((\mathcal{E}^{f}_{U|X})^{{\rm an}})\qquad\in\ {\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}({\rm I}\mathbb{C}_{X^{{\rm an}}}).

(5.8)

Then by Theorem
4.1
(vi) we have an isomorphism

Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{E}^{f}_{U|X})\simeq\mathbb{E}_{U^{{\rm an}}|X^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}f}.

(5.9)

Definition 5.1
.

We say that a holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module

\mathcal{M}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize hol}}(\mathcal{D}_{X})
is an exponentially twisted holonomic
D-module if there exist a regular holonomic

\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{N}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize rh}}(\mathcal{D}_{X})
and a rational function

f=\frac{P}{Q}:U=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
(
P,Q\in\Gamma(X;\mathcal{O}_{X}),Q\not=0
) on
X
such that we have an isomorphism

\mathcal{M}\simeq\mathcal{N}\overset{D}{\otimes}\mathcal{E}^{f}_{U|X}.

(5.10)

Let
\mathcal{M}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize hol}}(\mathcal{D}_{X})
be an
exponentially twisted holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module such that

\mathcal{M}\simeq\mathcal{N}\overset{D}{\otimes}\mathcal{E}^{f}_{U|X}

for a regular holonomic

\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{N}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize rh}}(\mathcal{D}_{X})
and a rational function

f=\frac{P}{Q}:U=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
on
X
. Let us set

K:=Sol_{X}(\mathcal{N})\qquad\in\ {\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X^{{\rm an}}).

(5.11)

We denote the support of
K\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X^{{\rm an}})

by
Z\subset X
. Note that
Z={\rm supp}(\mathcal{N})

and if
Z\subset Q^{-1}(0)
then we have

\mathcal{M}\simeq 0
. Hence in what follows, we assume
that
Z
is not contained in
Q^{-1}(0)
.
Then in this algebraic case,
considering
f
as a rational function on
a (possibly singular) compactification

\overline{Z}
of
Z
and using the enhanced
solution complex of
\mathcal{M}
, we see that the
restriction of
f
to
Z
is uniquely
determined by
\mathcal{M}
modulo constant functions
on
Z
. Note also that the shift
K[N]\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X^{{\rm an}})
of
K

is a perverse sheaf on
X^{{\rm an}}
. Then by
Theorem
4.1
(vi)
and Corollary
4.2
for the enhanced sheaf

F:=\pi^{-1}K\otimes{\rm E}_{U^{{\rm an}}|X^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}f}\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{+}(\mathbb{C}_{X^{{\rm an}}})

on
X^{{\rm an}}
we have
an isomorphism

Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\mathbb{C}^{\rm E}_{X^{{\rm an}}}\overset{+}{\otimes}F.

(5.12)

From now on, we shall study
the enhanced micro-support
{\rm SS}^{{\rm E}}(F)\subset(T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathbb{R}
and the irregular one

{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}(F)\subset T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}}
of the enhanced sheaf

F=\pi^{-1}K\otimes{\rm E}_{U^{{\rm an}}|X^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}f}\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{+}(\mathbb{C}_{X^{{\rm an}}})
.
It turns out that
over the open subset

T^{*}U_{\mathbb{R}}\subset T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}}
the structures
of these two micro-supports are very simple.
Define a (not necessarily
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-conic)
complex Lagrangian submanifold
\Lambda^{f}
of

T^{*}U
by

\Lambda^{f}:=\{(z,df(z))\ |\ z\in U=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)\}\qquad\subset T^{*}U.

(5.13)

Then we can easily show that
via the natural identification
(T^{*}U)_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq T^{*}U_{\mathbb{R}}
we have

{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}(F)\cap T^{*}U_{\mathbb{R}}=\Bigl({\rm SS}(K)\cap T^{*}U_{\mathbb{R}}\Bigr)+\Lambda^{f}

(5.14)

and

\displaystyle{\rm SS}^{{\rm E}}(F)\cap\Bigl((T^{*}U_{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathbb{R}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\Bigl\{(x,y;x^{*},y^{*},-{\rm Re}f(x+iy)\ |\ (x,y;x^{*},y^{*})\in\Bigl({\rm SS}(K)\cap T^{*}U_{\mathbb{R}}\Bigr)+\Lambda^{f}\Bigr\}.

Moreover we have the following results.

Lemma 5.2
.

We denote the preimage of the locally closed subset

Q^{-1}(0)\setminus I(f)=Q^{-1}(0)\setminus P^{-1}(0)\subset X

by the projection
(T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow X_{\mathbb{R}}
simply by

\{P\not=0,\ Q=0\}\subset(T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathbb{R}
. Then
we have

{\rm SS}^{{\rm E}}(F)\cap\{P\not=0,\ Q=0\}=\emptyset.

(5.15)

In particular, if we assume
that
I(f)=P^{-1}(0)\cap Q^{-1}(0)=\emptyset
,
then we have

{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}(F)=\Bigl({\rm SS}(K)\cap T^{*}U_{\mathbb{R}}\Bigr)+\Lambda^{f}.

(5.16)

Proof.

The problem being local, after shrinking
X
we may
assume that
I(f)=\emptyset
.
First, we consider the special case where
X=\mathbb{C}_{z}
,
K=\mathbb{C}_{X}
and
P(z)=1
,
Q(z)=z
,

f(z)=\frac{1}{z}
. In this case, we have

F=\mathbb{C}_{\{z\not=0,\ t+{\rm Re}(\frac{1}{z})\geq 0\}}.

(5.17)

Then it suffices to show that for any point

(0,t_{0})\in(Q^{-1}(0)\setminus I(f))\times\mathbb{R}=Q^{-1}(0)\times\mathbb{R}
there exists no covector
of the form
\xi dx+dt
in
T^{*}_{(0,t_{0})}(X_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R})\cap{\rm SS}(F)
. For such a point
(0,t_{0})

we can easily see that there exist its
neighborhoods
W,W^{\prime}
in
X_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}\simeq\mathbb{R}^{3}
and a diffeomorphism

\Phi:W\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}W^{\prime}
between them such that

\Phi((0,t_{0}))=(0,t_{0})
,

\Phi\Bigl(\{z\not=0,\ t+{\rm Re}\Bigl(\frac{1}{z}\Bigr)\geq 0\}\cap W\Bigr)=\{z\not=0,\ t_{0}+{\rm Re}\Bigl(\frac{1}{z}\Bigr)\geq 0\}\cap W^{\prime}

(5.18)

and the tangent map

T_{(0,t_{0})}\Phi:T_{(0,t_{0})}(X_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R})\longrightarrow T_{(0,t_{0})}(X_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R})

(5.19)

of
\Phi
at it is the identity. Then locally
we can replace
F
by the sheaf

F^{\prime}=\mathbb{C}_{\{z\not=0,\ t_{0}+{\rm Re}(\frac{1}{z})\geq 0\}}

(5.20)

to check the condition. The same argument can be applied
even if we replace
f(x)
by
\frac{1}{z^{m}}
(
m\geq 1
).
Next consider the case where

X=\mathbb{C}^{N}_{z}
,
K=\mathbb{C}_{X}
and
P(z)=1
,
Q(z)=z_{1}^{m_{1}}z_{2}^{m_{2}}\cdots z_{k}^{m_{k}}
(
m_{i}\geq 1
),

f(z)=\frac{1}{Q(z)}
for some
1\leq k\leq N={\rm dim}X
.
In this case, for the sheaf

F=\mathbb{C}_{\{Q(z)\not=0,\ t+{\rm Re}f(z)\geq 0\}}

(5.21)

on
X_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}

we can similarly check the condition.
Let us now consider the more general case where

K=\mathbb{C}_{X}
and
P(z)=1
,
Q(z)\not=0

f(z)=\frac{1}{Q(z)}
.
Let
\nu:\widetilde{X}\longrightarrow X
be a
proper morphism of complex manifolds such that
the restriction
\widetilde{X}\setminus\nu^{-1}Q^{-1}(0)\longrightarrow X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)
of
\nu

is an isomorphism and
\nu^{-1}Q^{-1}(0)\subset\widetilde{X}
is
a normal crossing divisor in
\widetilde{X}
. Then
for the sheaf
F
on
X_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}
and
the morphism
\widetilde{\nu}:=\nu\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{R}}:\widetilde{X}_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow X_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}

we have the condition

{\rm SS}^{{\rm E}}(\widetilde{\nu}^{-1}F)\cap\{P\circ\nu\not=0,\ Q\circ\nu=0\}=\emptyset

(5.22)

and there exists an isomorphism

F\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm R}\widetilde{\nu}_{*}\widetilde{\nu}^{-1}F.

(5.23)

Hence we can check the condition on
F
by
[
27
, Proposition 5.4.4]
.
Finally, we consider the general case.
Let
\mathcal{S}
be a stratification of

Z
adapted to
K
such that
Q^{-1}(0)\cap Z

is a union of some strata in it. Then by decomposing
the support of
K
with respect to
\mathcal{S}
, it suffices
to show that for any stratum
S\in\mathcal{S}
in
\mathcal{S}

such that
S\subset Z\setminus Q^{-1}(0)
and any
local system
L
on
S
we have the condition

{\rm SS}^{{\rm E}}\Bigl(\pi^{-1}(i_{!}L)\otimes{\rm E}_{U|X}^{{\rm Re}f}\Bigr)\cap\{P\not=0,\ Q=0\}=\emptyset,

(5.24)

where
i:S\hookrightarrow X
is the inclusion map.
Let
\nu:T\longrightarrow X
be a
proper morphism of complex manifolds such that

\nu(T)=\overline{S}
and
the restriction
T^{\circ}:=T\setminus\nu^{-1}(\overline{S}\setminus S)\longrightarrow S
of
\nu

is an isomorphism and
\nu^{-1}Q^{-1}(0),\nu^{-1}(\overline{S}\setminus S)\subset T
are
normal crossing divisors in
T
. We thus
can identify
T^{\circ}
with
S
and regard

L
as a local system on
T^{\circ}
.
Let us consider the meromorphic function

f\circ\nu:=(P\circ\nu)/(Q\circ\nu)
on
T
and the inclusion map

i^{\prime}:T^{\circ}\hookrightarrow T
.
Then for the sheaf

\pi^{-1}(i^{\prime}_{!}L)\otimes{\rm E}_{T^{\circ}|T}^{{\rm Re}(f\circ\nu)}

on
T_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}

we can similarly show the condition

{\rm SS}^{{\rm E}}\Bigl(\pi^{-1}(i^{\prime}_{!}L)\otimes{\rm E}_{T^{\circ}|T}^{{\rm Re}(f\circ\nu)}\Bigr)\cap\{P\circ\nu\not=0,\ Q\circ\nu=0\}=\emptyset.

(5.25)

Moreover, for the morphism

\widetilde{\nu}:=\nu\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{R}}:T_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow X_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}

there exists an isomorphism

\pi^{-1}(i_{!}L)\otimes{\rm E}_{U|X}^{{\rm Re}f}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm R}\widetilde{\nu}_{*}\Bigl(\pi^{-1}(i^{\prime}_{!}L)\otimes{\rm E}_{T^{\circ}|T}^{{\rm Re}(f\circ\nu)}\Bigr).

(5.26)

Then we obtain (
5.24
) by
[
27
, Proposition 5.4.4]
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

Assume that there exists a stratification
\mathcal{S}
of

Z
adapted to
K
such that any stratum
S\in\mathcal{S}

in it is not contained in
P^{-1}(0)\cap Z\subset Z
nor

Q^{-1}(0)\cap Z\subset Z
and the meromorphic
function
f|_{S\setminus Q^{-1}(0)}:S\setminus Q^{-1}(0)\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
on
S

satisfies the condition: For any point of

S\cap P^{-1}(0)\cap Q^{-1}(0)
there exists
a local coordinate
z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{l}

(
l:={\rm dim}S
) of
S
around it such that

(P|_{S})(z_{1},\ldots,z_{l})=z_{1},\ (Q|_{S})(z_{1},\ldots,z_{l})=z_{2}^{m}

for some
m\geq 1
and hence

(f|_{S})(z_{1},\ldots,z_{l})=\frac{z_{1}}{z_{2}^{m}}.

(5.27)

We denote the preimage of the closed subset

I(f)=P^{-1}(0)\cap Q^{-1}(0)\subset X

by the projection
(T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow X_{\mathbb{R}}
simply by

\{P=Q=0\}\subset(T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathbb{R}
. Then
we have

{\rm SS}^{{\rm E}}(F)\cap\{P=Q=0\}=\emptyset.

(5.28)

Proof.

The problem being local, we may assume that
locally
X=\mathbb{C}^{N}
.
First, we consider the special case where
N=2
,
Z=X=\mathbb{C}^{2}
,
K=\mathbb{C}_{X}
and
P(z_{1},z_{2})=z_{1}
,
Q(z_{1},z_{2})=z_{2}
,

f(z_{1},z_{2})=\frac{z_{1}}{z_{2}}
. We set
z=(z_{1},z_{2})=(x,y)

so that we have
f(x,y)=\frac{x}{y}
. We shall show

{\rm SS}^{{\rm E}}(F)\cap\{x=y=0\}=\emptyset
. By an explicit calculation, we can easily
show that the closure of the set

{\rm SS}^{{\rm E}}(F)\setminus\{x=y=0\}\subset(T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathbb{R}\setminus\{x=y=0\}

(5.29)

in
(T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathbb{R}
does not intersect

\{x=y=0\}
. Let
\nu:\widetilde{X}\longrightarrow X

be the blow-up of
X=\mathbb{C}^{2}
along the origin

\{(0,0)\}\subset X=\mathbb{C}^{2}
and

M=\mathbb{C}^{2}_{u,y}
the affine chart of
\widetilde{X}

such that we have

\nu|_{M}:M=\mathbb{C}^{2}_{u,y}\longrightarrow X=\mathbb{C}^{2}\qquad((u,y)\longmapsto(uy,y)).

(5.30)

Then for the exceptional divisor
\nu^{-1}(\{(0,0)\})\simeq{\mathbb{P}}:={\mathbb{P}}^{1}
we have
\nu^{-1}(\{(0,0)\})\cap M=\mathbb{C}_{u}\times\{0\}=\{y=0\}\subset M=\mathbb{C}^{2}_{u,y}

and
(f\circ\nu)(u,y)=\frac{uy}{y}=u
on
M=\mathbb{C}^{2}_{u,y}
.
Let
M^{\prime}=\mathbb{C}^{2}_{x,v}
be the affine chart of
\widetilde{X}

such that we have

\nu|_{M^{\prime}}:M^{\prime}=\mathbb{C}^{2}_{x,v}\longrightarrow X=\mathbb{C}^{2}\qquad((x,v)\longmapsto(x,vx)).

(5.31)

Then we have
M\cup M^{\prime}=\widetilde{X}
,

\nu^{-1}(\{(0,0)\})\cap M^{\prime}=\{0\}\times\mathbb{C}_{v}=\{x=0\}\subset M^{\prime}=\mathbb{C}^{2}_{x,v}

and
(f\circ\nu)(x,v)=\frac{x}{vx}=\frac{1}{v}
on
M^{\prime}=\mathbb{C}^{2}_{x,v}
.
Hence the meromorphic function
f\circ\nu

on
\widetilde{X}
has no point of indeterminacy
on the whole
\widetilde{X}
and its pole is
contained in
\widetilde{X}\setminus M=\{v=0\}\subset M^{\prime}=\mathbb{C}^{2}_{x,v}
.
Now we define an enhanced sheaf
\widetilde{F}
on

\widetilde{X}
by

\widetilde{F}:={\rm E}_{M\setminus\{y=0\}|\widetilde{X}}^{{\rm Re}(f\circ\nu)}\quad\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{+}(\mathbb{C}_{\widetilde{X}^{{\rm an}}})

(5.32)

and set
\widetilde{\nu}:=\nu\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{R}}:\widetilde{X}\times\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow X\times\mathbb{R}

so that there exists an isomorphism

{\rm R}\widetilde{\nu}_{*}\widetilde{F}\simeq F.

(5.33)

Let

T^{*}(X\times\mathbb{R})\overset{\varpi}{\longleftarrow}(\widetilde{X}\times\mathbb{R})\times_{(X\times\mathbb{R})}T^{*}(X\times\mathbb{R})\overset{\rho}{\longrightarrow}T^{*}(\widetilde{X}\times\mathbb{R})

(5.34)

be the natural morphisms associated to
\widetilde{\nu}
.
Then by
[
27
, Proposition 5.4.4]
we have

{\rm SS}(F)\subset\varpi\rho^{-1}{\rm SS}(\widetilde{F}).

(5.35)

Let

(0,0,t;\xi,\eta,1)\qquad\in\{t^{*}>0\}\subset T^{*}(X\times\mathbb{R})

(5.36)

be a point which corresponds to the covector

\xi dx+\eta dy+dt\in T^{*}_{(0,0,t)}(X\times\mathbb{R})

at the point
(0,0,t)\in\{P=Q=0\}\subset X\times\mathbb{R}
.
Then its pull-back by the map

\widetilde{\nu}|_{M\times\mathbb{R}}:M\times\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow X\times\mathbb{R}\qquad((u,y,t)\longmapsto(uy,y,t))

(5.37)

is calculated as

\bigl(\widetilde{\nu}|_{M\times\mathbb{R}}\bigr)^{*}(\xi dx+\eta dy+dt)=(\xi u+\eta)dy+dt.

(5.38)

Since
{\rm Re}(f\circ\nu)(u,y)={\rm Re}u
on
M=\mathbb{C}^{2}_{u,y}

and hence together with Lemma
5.2

there is no covector of the form

(\xi u+\eta)dy+dt
in

\{t^{*}=1\}\cap{\rm SS}(\widetilde{F})

over the exceptional divisor
\nu^{-1}(\{(0,0)\})
,
by (
5.35
) we obtain the assertion
{\rm SS}^{{\rm E}}(F)\cap\{x=y=0\}=\emptyset
. We can treat also the case
where
N=2
,
Z=X=\mathbb{C}^{2}
,
K=\mathbb{C}_{X}
and
P(z_{1},z_{2})=z_{1}
,
Q(z_{1},z_{2})=z_{2}^{m}
,

f(z_{1},z_{2})=\frac{z_{1}}{z_{2}^{m}}
for some

m\geq 2
by repeating blow-ups (see e.g.
the proof of
[
45
, Theorem 3.6]
).
The general case can be treated similarly by decomposing
the support of
K
with respect to the stratification

\mathcal{S}
. This completes the proof.
∎

By the natural identification
(T^{*}X)_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}}
we regard
T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}}
as a complex symplectic
manifold endowed with the canonical
holomorphic symplectic 2-form

\sigma_{X}
.

Lemma 5.4
.

The irregular micro-support

{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}(F)\subset T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}}
of

F=\pi^{-1}K\otimes{\rm E}_{U|Z}^{{\rm Re}f}\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{+}(\mathbb{C}_{X^{{\rm an}}})
is contained in a complex
isotropic analytic subset of

(T^{*}X)_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}}
.

Proof.

Let us consider the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow F_{\pi^{-1}(X\setminus P^{-1}(0))}\longrightarrow F\longrightarrow F_{\pi^{-1}(P^{-1}(0))}\longrightarrow 0

(5.39)

of enhanced sheaves on
X
. Since there exists an isomorphism

F_{\pi^{-1}(P^{-1}(0))}\simeq\pi^{-1}K\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\{(z,t)\in U\times\mathbb{R}\ |\ z\in U\cap P^{-1}(0),\ t\geq 0\}},

(5.40)

we see that
{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}(F_{\pi^{-1}(P^{-1}(0))})={\rm SS}(K_{U\cap P^{-1}(0)})
is a Lagrangian
analytic subset of
(T^{*}X)_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Let
\nu:\widetilde{X}\longrightarrow X
be a
proper morphism of complex manifolds such that
the restriction
\widetilde{X}\setminus\nu^{-1}(P^{-1}(0)\cup Q^{-1}(0))\longrightarrow X\setminus(P^{-1}(0)\cup Q^{-1}(0))
of
\nu

is an isomorphism,
\nu^{-1}(P^{-1}(0)\cup Q^{-1}(0))\subset\widetilde{X}
is
a normal crossing divisor, and the meromorphic function

f\circ\nu:=(P\circ\nu)/(Q\circ\nu)
on
\widetilde{X}
has
no point of indeterminacy on the whole
\widetilde{X}

(see e.g. the proof of
[
45
, Theorem 3.6]
).
We thus obtain a function
f\circ\nu:\nu^{-1}(U)=\widetilde{X}\setminus(Q\circ\nu)^{-1}(0)\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
.
Moreover we define a complex Lagrangian submanifold

\Lambda^{f\circ\nu}\subset T^{*}\widetilde{X}
by

\Lambda^{f\circ\nu}:=\{(z,d(f\circ\nu)(z))\ |\ z\in\nu^{-1}(U)\}\quad\subset T^{*}\widetilde{X}

(5.41)

and set
\widetilde{\nu}:=\nu\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{R}}:\widetilde{X}\times\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow X\times\mathbb{R}
.
Then for the enhanced sheaf
\widetilde{\nu}^{-1}F_{\pi^{-1}(X\setminus P^{-1}(0))}
on

\widetilde{X}
we can easily show that

{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}\bigl(\widetilde{\nu}^{-1}F_{\pi^{-1}(X\setminus P^{-1}(0))}\bigr)={\rm SS}\bigl(\nu^{-1}K_{U\setminus P^{-1}(0)}\bigr)+\Lambda^{f\circ\nu}

(5.42)

in
T^{*}\widetilde{X}
. This implies that
{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}\bigl(\widetilde{\nu}^{-1}F_{\pi^{-1}(X\setminus P^{-1}(0))}\bigr)
is a Lagrangian
analytic subset of
T^{*}\widetilde{X}
. Let

T^{*}X\overset{\varpi}{\longleftarrow}\widetilde{X}\times_{X}T^{*}X\overset{\rho}{\longrightarrow}T^{*}\widetilde{X}

(5.43)

be the natural morphisms associated to

\nu:\widetilde{X}\longrightarrow X
.
Since
\nu
is proper and there exists
an isomorphism

F_{\pi^{-1}(X\setminus P^{-1}(0))}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm R}\widetilde{\nu}_{*}\widetilde{\nu}^{-1}(F_{\pi^{-1}(X\setminus P^{-1}(0))}),

(5.44)

by
[
27
, Proposition 5.4.4]
we have

{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}(F_{\pi^{-1}(X\setminus P^{-1}(0))})\subset\varpi\rho^{-1}{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}\bigl(\widetilde{\nu}^{-1}F_{\pi^{-1}(X\setminus P^{-1}(0))}\bigr).

(5.45)

It is clear that the right hand side is an analytic
subset of
T^{*}X
. Moreover, by

[
15
, page 43]
there exist some
stratifications of it and
\rho^{-1}{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}\bigl(\widetilde{\nu}^{-1}F_{\pi^{-1}(X\setminus P^{-1}(0))}\bigr)
such that the morphism

\rho^{-1}{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}\bigl(\widetilde{\nu}^{-1}F_{\pi^{-1}(X\setminus P^{-1}(0))}\bigr)\longrightarrow\varpi\rho^{-1}{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}\bigl(\widetilde{\nu}^{-1}F_{\pi^{-1}(X\setminus P^{-1}(0))}\bigr)

(5.46)

induced by
\varpi
is a
stratified fiber bundle over
each stratum. Then we can slightly modify the proof
of
[
24
, Proposition A.54]
to show that
the symplectic 2-form

\sigma_{X}
vanishes on

\varpi\rho^{-1}{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}\bigl(\widetilde{\nu}^{-1}F_{\pi^{-1}(X\setminus P^{-1}(0))}\bigr)
. We thus
obtain the assertion.
∎

In the special case where
X
is

\mathbb{C}^{N}_{z}
,
Y
is its
dual vector space
\mathbb{C}^{N}_{w}
and

T^{*}X\simeq X\times Y
, we shall
introduce a new subset of
(T^{*}X)_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}}
as follows.
For a point
w_{0}\in Y=\mathbb{C}^{N}
we define a
rational function
f^{w_{0}}
on
X=\mathbb{C}^{N}
by

f^{w_{0}}:U=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}\qquad(z\longmapsto\langle z,w_{0}\rangle-f(z)).

(5.47)

Definition 5.5
.

In the case where
X
is

\mathbb{C}^{N}_{z}
,
Y
is its
dual vector space
\mathbb{C}^{N}_{w}
and

T^{*}X\simeq X\times Y
, by using
K
and
f
in
F=\pi^{-1}K\otimes{\rm E}_{U^{{\rm an}}|X^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}f}

we define a subset

{\rm SS}_{{\rm eva}}(F)\subset(T^{*}X)_{\mathbb{R}}
of
(T^{*}X)_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}}
by

{\rm SS}_{{\rm eva}}(F):=\{(z_{0},w_{0})\in(T^{*}X)_{\mathbb{R}}\ |\ \phi^{{\rm mero,c}}_{f^{w_{0}}-c}(K)_{z_{0}}\not=0\ \text{for some}\ c\in\mathbb{C}\}.

(5.48)

Unfortunately, we do not know if this set

{\rm SS}_{{\rm eva}}(F)\subset T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}}
is contained in the
irregular micro-support
{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}(F)
of
F
or not. With Lemmas
5.2
,

5.3
and
5.4
at hands,
now we have obtained a rough picture of the irregular
micro-support
{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}(F)\subset T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}}
.
However, for the moment it is not clear for us if it
is a complex Lagrangian analytic subset
of
(T^{*}X)_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}}
or not.
For these reasons, we define a (not necessarily
homogeneous) complex Lagrangian analytic
subset of
(T^{*}X)_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}}

in a different way
as follows and use it instead of
{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}(F)
.
We return to the case where
X
is a smooth
algebraic variety over
\mathbb{C}
.
First, as a complex analogue of the

\mathbb{R}
-constructible sheaf

F=\pi^{-1}K\otimes{\rm E}_{U^{{\rm an}}|X^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}f}

on
X_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}
, by applying
the proof of Theorem
2.4
(i) to
the (not necessarily) closed embedding

i_{-f}:U=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)\hookrightarrow X\times\mathbb{C},\qquad(z\longmapsto(z,-f(z)))

(5.49)

associated to the function

-f:U\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
, we obtain a complex
constructible sheaf

\mathcal{F}:=(i_{-f})_{!}(K|_{U})\quad\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X\times\mathbb{C})

(5.50)

on
X\times\mathbb{C}
.
Then for the morphism
{\rm id}_{X}\times{\rm Re}:X\times\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow X\times\mathbb{R}
induced by
the one
{\rm Re}:\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}

(
\tau\longmapsto{\rm Re}\tau
)
we have an isomorphism

F\simeq\mathbb{C}_{\{t\geq 0\}}\overset{+}{\otimes}{\rm R}({\rm id}_{X}\times{\rm Re})_{!}\mathcal{F}.

(5.51)

Note that
\mathcal{F}[N]
is a perverse
sheaf and its micro-support

{\rm SS}(\mathcal{F}[N])={\rm SS}(\mathcal{F})

is a homogeneous complex Lagrangian
analytic subset of
T^{*}(X\times\mathbb{C})
.
Then as in the definitions of
{\rm SS}^{{\rm E}}(F)\subset(T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathbb{R}

and
{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}(F)\subset T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}}
,
by forgetting the homogeneity of
{\rm SS}(\mathcal{F})

we define two subsets:

{\rm SS}^{{\rm E},\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})\subset T^{*}X\times\mathbb{C},\qquad{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})\subset T^{*}X

(5.52)

in the following way. First, by the closed embedding

\iota:(T^{*}X)\times\mathbb{C}\hookrightarrow T^{*}(X\times\mathbb{C})\qquad(((z,w),\tau)\longmapsto(z,\tau;w,1))

(5.53)

we set

{\rm SS}^{{\rm E},\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F}):=\iota^{-1}{\rm SS}(\mathcal{F})\ \subset\ (T^{*}X)\times\mathbb{C}.

(5.54)

It is clear that
{\rm SS}^{{\rm E},\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})

is a complex analytic subset of
(T^{*}X)\times\mathbb{C}
.
Let us call it the enhanced micro-support of

\mathcal{F}\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X\times\mathbb{C})
. Then we
define
{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})\subset T^{*}X

to be the closure of the image of

{\rm SS}^{{\rm E},\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})
by the
projection
(T^{*}X)\times\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow T^{*}X
.
We call it the irregular micro-support of
\mathcal{F}
.
As the restriction of
f
to
Z
is uniquely
determined by
\mathcal{M}
modulo constant functions
on
Z
, we see that

{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})
does
not depend on the expression
\mathcal{N}\overset{D}{\otimes}\mathcal{E}^{f}_{U|X}

of
\mathcal{M}
. It is easy to see that the results
analogous to Lemmas
5.2
and

5.3

holds true also for
{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})\subset T^{*}X
.

Lemma 5.6
.

The irregular micro-support

{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})\subset T^{*}X
of

\mathcal{F}\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X\times\mathbb{C})
is a complex
Lagrangian analytic subset of

(T^{*}X)_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq T^{*}X_{\mathbb{R}}
. If we assume moreover
that
I(f)=P^{-1}(0)\cap Q^{-1}(0)=\emptyset
,
then we have

{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})=\bigl({\rm SS}(K)\cap T^{*}U_{\mathbb{R}}\bigr)+\Lambda^{f}.

(5.55)

Proof.

By the inclusion map
j:X\times\mathbb{C}\hookrightarrow X\times{\mathbb{P}}
we obtain an algebraic constructible
sheaf

j_{!}\mathcal{F}=j_{!}(i_{-f})_{!}(K|_{U})\quad\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X\times{\mathbb{P}})

(5.56)

on
X\times{\mathbb{P}}
. Then by the theorem
in
[
15
, page 43]
there exist Whitney
stratifications
\mathcal{S}
and
\mathcal{S}_{0}
of

X\times{\mathbb{P}}
and
X
respectively such that

{\rm SS}(j_{!}\mathcal{F})\subset\bigsqcup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}T^{*}_{S}(X\times{\mathbb{P}}),

(5.57)

X\times\mathbb{C}
is a union of some strata in
\mathcal{S}

and the projection
X\times{\mathbb{P}}\longrightarrow X
is a stratified fiber
bundle as in its assertion.
Note that for each stratum
S_{0}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}
in
\mathcal{S}_{0}

there exist only finitely many strata

S\in\mathcal{S}
in
\mathcal{S}
projecting to it such
that
S\subset X\times\mathbb{C}
,

{\rm dim}S={\rm dim}S_{0}
and
the enhanced micro-support
{\rm SS}^{{\rm E},\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})\subset(T^{*}X)\times\mathbb{C}
of
\mathcal{F}
is
determined only by the conormal bundles

T^{*}_{S}(X\times{\mathbb{P}})
of such “horizontal”
strata
S\in\mathcal{S}
such that
S\subset X\times\mathbb{C}
. Then we obtain the first assertion
by simple calculations. We obtain also the second one
as in the proof of Lemma
5.2
.
∎

By the proof of Lemma
5.6
,
considering also the multiplicities of
\mathcal{F}

we obtain a Lagrangian cycle in

T^{*}X
supported by
the irregular micro-support

{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})\subset T^{*}X

of
\mathcal{F}
. As it depends only on
\mathcal{M}
,
we call it the irregular characteristic
cycle of
\mathcal{M}
and denote it by
{\rm CC}_{{\rm irr}}(\mathcal{M})
.
Moreover it satisfies also the following
functorial property.
Let
\phi:X\longrightarrow Y
be
a proper morphism of smooth algebraic varieties and

\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:={\mathbf{D}}\phi_{\ast}\mathcal{M}\in{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\rm hol}(\mathcal{D}_{Y})

the direct image of the exponentially
twisted holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{M}
by it.
Then by Theorem
4.1
(iii) for the
enhanced sheaf
F^{\prime}:=\mathbf{E}\phi_{*}F[d_{X}-d_{Y}]\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{+}(\mathbb{C}_{Y^{{\rm an}}})
on
Y^{{\rm an}}

we have an isomorphism

Sol_{Y}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M}^{\prime})\simeq\mathbb{C}^{\rm E}_{Y^{{\rm an}}}\overset{+}{\otimes}F^{\prime}.

(5.58)

Similarly to the case of
\mathcal{M}
,
also for
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}={\mathbf{D}}\phi_{\ast}\mathcal{M}

we can define a Lagrangian cycle in
T^{*}Y

by using the complex constructible sheaf

\mathcal{F}^{\prime}:={\rm R}(\phi\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{C}})_{*}\mathcal{F}[d_{X}-d_{Y}]\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(Y\times\mathbb{C})
. Denote it by

{\rm CC}_{{\rm irr}}(\mathcal{M}^{\prime})
and let

T^{*}Y\overset{\varpi}{\longleftarrow}X\times_{Y}T^{*}Y\overset{\rho}{\longrightarrow}T^{*}X

(5.59)

be the natural morphisms associated to

\phi:X\longrightarrow Y
. Then there
exists an equality

{\rm CC}_{{\rm irr}}(\mathcal{M}^{\prime})=\varpi_{*}\rho^{*}{\rm CC}_{{\rm irr}}(\mathcal{M})

(5.60)

of Lagrangian cycles in
T^{*}Y
(see
[
27
, Proposition 9.4.2]
).
Note also that by the commutative diagram

\begin{CD}X\times\mathbb{C}@>{{\rm id}_{X}\times{\rm Re}}>{}>X\times\mathbb{R}\\
@V{\phi\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{C}}}V{}V@V{}V{\phi\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{R}}}V\\
Y\times\mathbb{C}@>{{\rm id}_{Y}\times{\rm Re}}>{}>Y\times\mathbb{R}\end{CD}

(5.61)

we obtain an isomorphism

F^{\prime}\simeq\mathbb{C}_{\{t\geq 0\}}\overset{+}{\otimes}{\rm R}({\rm id}_{Y}\times{\rm Re})_{!}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}.

(5.62)

Also for a morphism
\psi:Y\longrightarrow X

of smooth algebraic varieties and the inverse image

\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:={\mathbf{D}}\psi^{\ast}\mathcal{M}\in{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\rm hol}(\mathcal{D}_{Y})

of
\mathcal{M}
by it,
we can define a Lagrangian cycle

{\rm CC}_{{\rm irr}}(\mathcal{M}^{\prime})
in
T^{*}Y

and obtain a formula which expresses it
in terms of
{\rm CC}_{{\rm irr}}(\mathcal{M})

under some non-characteristic condition.
Note that in this case the inverse image

\mathcal{M}^{\prime}={\mathbf{D}}\psi^{\ast}\mathcal{M}
is
also of exponentially twisted type.

Proposition 5.7
.

In the case where
X
is

\mathbb{C}^{N}_{z}
,
Y
is its
dual vector space
\mathbb{C}^{N}_{w}
and

T^{*}X\simeq X\times Y
,
for a point
(z_{0},w_{0})
of
T^{*}X\simeq X\times Y

assume that there exists a complex number
c\in\mathbb{C}
such that

\phi^{{\rm mero,c}}_{f^{w_{0}}-c}(K)_{z_{0}}\not=0

(5.63)

and set
\tau_{0}:=c-\langle z_{0},w_{0}\rangle\in\mathbb{C}
.
Then we have
(z_{0},w_{0},\tau_{0})\in{\rm SS}^{{\rm E},\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})
, equivalently

(z_{0},\tau_{0},w_{0},1)\in{\rm SS}(\mathcal{F})
.

Proof.

Let us consider
the (not necessarily) closed embedding

i_{f^{w_{0}}}:U=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)\hookrightarrow X\times\mathbb{C},\qquad(z\longmapsto(z,f^{w_{0}}(z)))

(5.64)

associated to the rational function

f^{w_{0}}:U\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
. Then we obtain
a non-vanishing

\phi_{\tau-c}\bigl((i_{f^{w_{0}}})_{!}(K|_{U})\bigr)_{(z_{0},c)}\simeq\phi^{{\rm mero,c}}_{f^{w_{0}}-c}(K)_{z_{0}}\not=0.

(5.65)

By
[
27
, Proposition 8.6.3]
,
this implies that the point

(z_{0},c,0,1)\in T^{*}(X\times\mathbb{C})
is
contained in
{\rm SS}\bigl((i_{f^{w_{0}}})_{!}(K|_{U})\bigr)\subset T^{*}(X\times\mathbb{C})
.
On the other hand, for the
automorphism
T_{w_{0}}
of
X\times\mathbb{C}
defined by

T_{w_{0}}:X\times\mathbb{C}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}X\times\mathbb{C},\qquad((z,\tau)\longmapsto(z,\tau+\langle z,w_{0}\rangle)),

(5.66)

we have an isomorphism

(T_{w_{0}})_{*}(\mathcal{F})=(T_{w_{0}})_{*}(i_{-f})_{!}(K|_{U})\simeq(i_{f^{w_{0}}})_{!}(K|_{U}).

(5.67)

Then the assertion
(z_{0},\tau_{0},w_{0},1)\in{\rm SS}(\mathcal{F})
immediately follows from it.
∎

By Proposition
5.7
the subset

{\rm SS}_{{\rm eva}}(F)\subset(T^{*}X)_{\mathbb{R}}
is contained in the
irregular micro-support

{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})

of
\mathcal{F}\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X\times\mathbb{C})
.
It is also clear that we have

{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})\cap T^{*}U_{\mathbb{R}}=\bigl({\rm SS}(K)\cap T^{*}U_{\mathbb{R}}\bigr)+\Lambda^{f}.

(5.68)

6
Fourier Transforms of Exponentially Twisted

Holonomic D-modules

In this section, we study the Fourier transforms of
exponentially twisted holonomic
D-modules. We inherit the situation
and the notations in
Section
1
. Let

X\overset{p}{\longleftarrow}X\times Y\overset{q}{\longrightarrow}Y

(6.1)

be the projections.
Then by Katz-Laumon
[
34
]
, for an
algebraic holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module

\mathcal{M}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize hol}}(\mathcal{D}_{X})
we have an isomorphism

\mathcal{M}^{\wedge}\simeq\mathbf{D}q_{\ast}(\mathbf{D}p^{\ast}\mathcal{M}\overset{D}{\otimes}\mathcal{O}_{X\times Y}e^{-\langle z,w\rangle}),

(6.2)

where
\mathbf{D}p^{\ast},\mathbf{D}q_{\ast},\overset{D}{\otimes}
are
the operations for algebraic D-modules
and
\mathcal{O}_{X\times Y}e^{-\langle z,w\rangle}
is the integral connection
of rank one on
X\times Y
associated to the canonical
paring
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle:X\times Y\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
.
In particular the right hand side is concentrated in degree zero.
Let
\overline{X}\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{N}
(resp.
\overline{Y}\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{N}
)
be the projective compactification of
X
(resp.
Y
).
By the inclusion map
i_{X}:X=\mathbb{C}^{N}\xhookrightarrow{\ \ \ }\overline{X}={\mathbb{P}}^{N}

we extend a holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{M}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize hol}}(\mathcal{D}_{X})

on
X
to the one
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}:=i_{X\ast}\mathcal{M}\simeq\mathbf{D}i_{X\ast}\mathcal{M}

on
\overline{X}
.
Denote by
\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}
the underlying complex manifold
of
\overline{X}
and define the analytification

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{{\ }{\rm an}}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize hol}}(\mathcal{D}_{\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}})

of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
by

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{{\ }{\rm an}}:=\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
.
Then we set

Sol_{\overline{X}}^{\rm E}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}):=Sol_{\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}}^{\rm E}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{{\ }{\rm an}})\ \in\ {\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}({\rm I}\mathbb{C}_{\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}}).

(6.3)

Similarly for the Fourier transform
\mathcal{M}^{\wedge}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize hol}}(\mathcal{D}_{Y})
,
by the inclusion map
i_{Y}:Y=\mathbb{C}^{N}\xhookrightarrow{\ \ \ }\overline{Y}={\mathbb{P}}^{N}

we define
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}^{\wedge}}
and

Sol_{\overline{Y}}^{\rm E}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}^{\wedge}})\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}({\rm I}\mathbb{C}_{\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}})
.
Let

\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}\overset{\overline{p}}{\longleftarrow}\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}\times\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}\overset{\overline{q}}{\longrightarrow}\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}

(6.4)

be the projections.
Then the following theorem is essentially due to
Kashiwara-Schapira
[
32
]
and D’Agnolo-Kashiwara
[
10
]
.
For
F\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}({\rm I}\mathbb{C}_{\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}})
we set

{}^{\rm L}F:=\mathbf{E}\overline{q}_{*}(\mathbf{E}\overline{p}^{-1}F\overset{+}{\otimes}\mathbb{E}_{X\times Y|\overline{X}\times\overline{Y}}^{-{\rm Re}\langle z,w\rangle}[N])\ \in\ {\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}({\rm I}\mathbb{C}_{\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}})

(6.5)

(here we denote
X^{{\rm an}}\times Y^{{\rm an}}
etc.
by
X\times Y
etc. for short) and call
it the Fourier-Sato (Fourier-Laplace) transform of
F
.

Theorem 6.1
.

For
\mathcal{M}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\rm hol}(\mathcal{D}_{X})
there exists an isomorphism

Sol_{\overline{Y}}^{\rm E}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}^{\wedge}})\simeq{}^{\rm L}Sol_{\overline{X}}^{\rm E}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}).

(6.6)

By
[
8
, Lemma 2.5.1]
we can take an
enhanced sheaf
F\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{+}(\mathbb{C}_{X^{{\rm an}}})
on

X^{{\rm an}}
such that
F\simeq\mathbb{C}_{\{t\geq 0\}}\overset{+}{\otimes}F
and

Sol_{\overline{X}}^{\rm E}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}})\simeq\mathbb{C}^{\rm E}_{\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}}\overset{+}{\otimes}\mathbf{E}i_{X!}F.

(6.7)

For an enhanced sheaf
G\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}})

on
\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}
we define its Fourier-Sato
(Fourier-Laplace) transform

{}^{\rm L}G\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}})
by

{}^{\rm L}G:=\mathbf{E}\overline{q}_{*}(\mathbf{E}\overline{p}^{-1}G\overset{+}{\otimes}{\rm E}_{X\times Y|\overline{X}\times\overline{Y}}^{-{\rm Re}\langle z,w\rangle}[N])\ \in\ {\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}}).

(6.8)

Since by
[
9
, Proposition 4.7.17]

we have

{}^{\rm L}(\mathbb{C}^{\rm E}_{\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}}\overset{+}{\otimes}(\cdot))\simeq\mathbb{C}^{\rm E}_{\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}}\overset{+}{\otimes}{}^{\rm L}(\cdot),

(6.9)

by Theorem
6.1
and (
6.7
)
we obtain an isomorphism

Sol_{\overline{Y}}^{\rm E}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}^{\wedge}})\simeq\mathbb{C}^{\rm E}_{\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}}\overset{+}{\otimes}{}^{\rm L}(\mathbf{E}i_{X!}F).

(6.10)

Hence it suffices to study the enhanced sheaf

{}^{\rm L}(\mathbf{E}i_{X!}F)\simeq\mathbf{E}i_{Y!}\mathbf{E}q_{!}(\mathbf{E}p^{-1}F\overset{+}{\otimes}{\rm E}_{X\times Y|X\times Y}^{-{\rm Re}\langle z,w\rangle}[N])\ \in\ {\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}})

(6.11)

on
\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}
. As was explained in

[
10
, Sections 2.6 and 7.3]
,
we can know the geometric structure of

L(F):=\mathbf{E}q_{!}(\mathbf{E}p^{-1}F\overset{+}{\otimes}{\rm E}_{X\times Y|X\times Y}^{-{\rm Re}\langle z,w\rangle}[N])\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{Y^{{\rm an}}})

to some extent by Tamarkin’s
microlocal thery of sheaves in
[
67
]
as follows.
Since we have

L(F)\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{+}(\mathbb{C}_{Y^{{\rm an}}})
we can define

{\rm SS}^{{\rm E}}(L(F))\subset(T^{*}Y_{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathbb{R}
and

{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}(L(F))\subset T^{*}Y_{\mathbb{R}}
also for
L(F)
.
Then we have the following theorem
due to Tamarkin
[
67
]
.
Let us consider the maps

\widetilde{\chi}:(T^{*}X^{{\rm an}})\times\mathbb{R}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(T^{*}Y^{{\rm an}})\times\mathbb{R},\quad((z,w),t)\longmapsto((w,-z),t+{\rm Re}\langle z,w\rangle)

(6.12)

and

\chi:T^{*}X^{{\rm an}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}T^{*}Y^{{\rm an}},\quad(z,w)\longmapsto(w,-z)).

(6.13)

Theorem 6.2

(
[
67
, Theorem 3.6]
)
.

For
F\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{+}(\mathbb{C}_{X^{{\rm an}}})
and

L(F)=\mathbf{E}q_{!}(\mathbf{E}p^{-1}F\overset{+}{\otimes}{\rm E}_{X\times Y|X\times Y}^{-{\rm Re}\langle z,w\rangle}[N])\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{+}(\mathbb{C}_{Y^{{\rm an}}})

we have

{\rm SS}^{{\rm E}}(L(F))=\widetilde{\chi}({\rm SS}^{{\rm E}}(F)),\quad{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}(L(F))=\chi({\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}(F)).

(6.14)

For a rational function

f=\frac{P}{Q}:X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
(
P,Q\in\Gamma(X;\mathcal{O}_{X})\simeq\mathbb{C}[z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{N}]
,

Q\not=0
) on
X=\mathbb{C}^{N}_{z}
we set

U:=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)
and
define an
exponential
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{E}^{f}_{U|X}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize hol}}(\mathcal{D}_{X})
as in the analytic case.
Then by Theorem
4.1
(vi)
for its extension

(\mathcal{E}^{f}_{U|X})^{\sim}:=i_{X\ast}\mathcal{E}^{f}_{U|X}\simeq\mathbf{D}i_{X\ast}\mathcal{E}^{f}_{U|X}

to
\overline{X}={\mathbb{P}}^{N}
we have an isomorphism

Sol_{\overline{X}}^{\rm E}((\mathcal{E}^{f}_{U|X})^{\sim})\simeq\mathbb{E}_{U^{{\rm an}}|\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}f}.

(6.15)

From now on, we fix an
exponentially twisted holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module

\mathcal{M}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize hol}}(\mathcal{D}_{X})
such that

\mathcal{M}\simeq\mathcal{N}\overset{D}{\otimes}\mathcal{E}^{f}_{U|X}

for a regular holonomic

\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{N}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize rh}}(\mathcal{D}_{X})
and a rational function

f=\frac{P}{Q}:U=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
on
X
and study
the basic properties of its
Fourier transform
\mathcal{M}^{\wedge}
. Let us set

K:=Sol_{X}(\mathcal{N})\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X^{{\rm an}}).

(6.16)

Note that the shift
K[N]\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X^{{\rm an}})
of
K

is a perverse sheaf on
X^{{\rm an}}
.
We denote the support of
K\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X^{{\rm an}})

by
Z\subset X
. Then by
Theorem
4.1
(vi)
and Corollary
4.2
there exists
an isomorphism

Sol_{\overline{X}}^{\rm E}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}})\simeq\bigl\{\pi^{-1}(i_{X})_{!}K\bigr\}\otimes\mathbb{E}_{U^{{\rm an}}|\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}f}.

(6.17)

Moreover
for the enhanced sheaf

\bigl\{\pi^{-1}(i_{X})_{!}K\bigr\}\otimes{\rm E}_{U^{{\rm an}}|\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}f}\simeq\mathbf{E}i_{X!}\Bigl\{\pi^{-1}K\otimes{\rm E}_{U^{{\rm an}}|X^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}f}\Bigr\}\qquad\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{+}(\mathbb{C}_{\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}})

(6.18)

on
\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}
we have an isomorphism

Sol_{\overline{X}}^{\rm E}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}})\simeq\mathbb{C}^{\rm E}_{\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}}\overset{+}{\otimes}\Bigl\{\bigl\{\pi^{-1}(i_{X})_{!}K\bigr\}\otimes{\rm E}_{U^{{\rm an}}|\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}f}\Bigr\}.

(6.19)

It follows from Theorem
6.1
that there exists
an isomorphism

Sol_{\overline{Y}}^{\rm E}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}^{\wedge}})\simeq\mathbb{C}^{\rm E}_{\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}}\overset{+}{\otimes}{}^{\rm L}\Bigl\{\bigl\{\pi^{-1}(i_{X})_{!}K\bigr\}\otimes{\rm E}_{U^{{\rm an}}|\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}f}\Bigr\}.

(6.20)

Hence it suffices to study the enhanced sheaf

{}^{\rm L}\Bigl\{\bigl\{\pi^{-1}(i_{X})_{!}K\bigr\}\otimes{\rm E}_{U^{{\rm an}}|\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}f}\Bigr\}\ \in\ {\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}}).

(6.21)

From now on, for the enhanced sheaf

F:=\pi^{-1}K\otimes{\rm E}_{U^{{\rm an}}|X^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}f}\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{+}(\mathbb{C}_{X^{{\rm an}}})

(6.22)

on
X_{\mathbb{R}}
we shall study

L(F)=\mathbf{E}q_{!}(\mathbf{E}p^{-1}F\overset{+}{\otimes}{\rm E}_{X\times Y|X\times Y}^{-{\rm Re}\langle z,w\rangle}[N])\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{+}(\mathbb{C}_{Y^{{\rm an}}})
.
For this purpose, as a complex analogue of the

\mathbb{R}
-constructible sheaf

F=\pi^{-1}K\otimes{\rm E}_{U^{{\rm an}}|X^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}f}

on
X_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}
, by applying
the proof of Theorem
2.4
(i) to
the (not necessarily) closed embedding

i_{-f}:U=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)\hookrightarrow X\times\mathbb{C},\qquad(z\longmapsto(z,-f(z)))

(6.23)

associated to the rational function

-f:U\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
, we obtain a complex
constructible sheaf

\mathcal{F}:=(i_{-f})_{!}(K|_{U})\quad\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X\times\mathbb{C})

(6.24)

on
X\times\mathbb{C}
. Note that
\mathcal{F}[N]
is a perverse
sheaf and its micro-support

{\rm SS}(\mathcal{F}[N])={\rm SS}(\mathcal{F})

is a homogeneous complex Lagrangian
analytic subset of
T^{*}(X\times\mathbb{C})
.
Then by forgetting the homogeneity of
{\rm SS}(\mathcal{F})

we define two subsets:

{\rm SS}^{{\rm E},\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})\subset T^{*}X\times\mathbb{C},\qquad{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})\subset T^{*}X

(6.25)

(see Section
5
for the details).
Recall that the irregular micro-support

{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})
of
\mathcal{F}\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X\times\mathbb{C})

is a (not necessarily homogeneous)
complex Lagrangian analytic subset of
T^{*}X
and

{\rm SS}_{{\rm eva}}(F)\subset(T^{*}X)_{\mathbb{R}}
is contained in

{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})
.
In what follows, we set
\Lambda:={\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})\subset(T^{*}X)_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Recall that we have

\Lambda\cap T^{*}U_{\mathbb{R}}={\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})\cap T^{*}U_{\mathbb{R}}=\bigl({\rm SS}(K)\cap T^{*}U_{\mathbb{R}}\bigr)+\Lambda^{f}.

(6.26)

Since
\Lambda
is Lagrangian and

{\rm dim}\Lambda={\rm dim}X=N
,
by e.g. the theorem in
[
15
, page 43]
we can
easily prove the following lemma.

Lemma 6.3
.

There exists a non-empty
Zariski open subset
\Omega\subset Y=\mathbb{C}_{w}^{N}

of
Y
such that the restriction
q^{-1}(\Omega)\cap\Lambda\longrightarrow\Omega
of the projection

q:X\times Y\longrightarrow Y
is
an unramified finite covering and any connected component
of the open subset
q^{-1}(\Omega)\cap\Lambda\subset\Lambda
is a fiber bundle over a
complex submanifold of
X=\mathbb{C}^{N}_{z}
contained
in
U=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)
or

I(f)=P^{-1}(0)\cap Q^{-1}(0)
.

Remark 6.4
.

In the special case where
I(f)=P^{-1}(0)\cap Q^{-1}(0)=\emptyset
, we have
\Lambda=\bigl({\rm SS}(K)\cap T^{*}U_{\mathbb{R}}\bigr)+\Lambda^{f}

by Lemma
5.6
and hence there exists a
(unique) maximal Zariski open subset

\Omega\subset Y=\mathbb{C}^{N}
satisfying the
conditions in Lemma
6.3
.

Let
\Omega\subset Y=\mathbb{C}_{w}^{N}
be as in
Lemma
6.3
and
V\subset\Omega

a contractible open subset of it.
Then for the decomposition

q^{-1}(V)\cap\Lambda=\Lambda_{V,1}\sqcup\Lambda_{V,2}\sqcup\cdots\cdots\sqcup\Lambda_{V,d}

(6.27)

of
q^{-1}(V)\cap\Lambda
into its connected components

\Lambda_{V,i}\subset\Lambda

(
1\leq i\leq d
)
the morphism
q|_{\Lambda}
induces an isomorphism

\Lambda_{V,i}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}V
for any
1\leq i\leq d
.
Now let us consider the symplectic transformation
of
[
10
]
:

\chi:T^{*}X\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}T^{*}Y\qquad((z,w)\longmapsto(w,-z)),

(6.28)

where we used the natural identification
T^{*}Y\simeq Y\times X
.
Then
\chi(\Lambda_{V,i})\subset T^{*}Y
is a
Lagrangian submanifold of
T^{*}Y
for which
the natural
projection
T^{*}Y\longrightarrow Y
induces an isomorphism

\chi(\Lambda_{V,i})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}V
. It follows that
there exists a holomorphic function
g_{i}
on
V
such that

\chi(\Lambda_{V,i})=\Lambda^{g_{i}}:=\{(w,dg_{i}(w))\ |\ w\in V\}\subset T^{*}Y.

(6.29)

Namely, if for a point
w\in V
we denote by

\zeta^{(i)}(w)=(\zeta^{(i)}_{1}(w),\zeta^{(i)}_{2}(w),\ldots,\zeta^{(i)}_{N}(w))\in X=\mathbb{C}^{N}_{z}
the
unique point of
X
such that
(\zeta^{(i)}(w),w)\in\Lambda_{V,i}\subset\Lambda
, then we have

\Bigl(\frac{\partial g_{i}}{\partial w_{1}}(w),\frac{\partial g_{i}}{\partial w_{2}}(w),\ldots\ldots,\frac{\partial g_{i}}{\partial w_{N}}(w)\Bigr)=-(\zeta^{(i)}_{1}(w),\zeta^{(i)}_{2}(w),\ldots,\zeta^{(i)}_{N}(w)).

(6.30)

More precisely, we have the following higher-dimensional
analogue of
[
10
, Lemma-Definition 7.4.2]
.
For
1\leq i\leq d
by our choice of
\Omega\subset Y=\mathbb{C}^{N}
in Lemma
6.3
,

Z_{i}:=p(\Lambda_{V,i})\subset X=\mathbb{C}^{N}_{z}
is a
complex submanifold of
X
.
We renumber them so that
for some
0\leq r\leq d
we have

Z_{i}\subset U=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)
(resp.

Z_{i}\subset I(f)=P^{-1}(0)\cap Q^{-1}(0)
) if
1\leq i\leq r

(resp. if
r+1\leq i\leq d
).

Lemma 6.5
.

For any
1\leq i\leq r
there exists a unique
holomorphic function
g_{i}
on
V\subset\Omega

such that
\chi(\Lambda_{V,i})=\Lambda^{g_{i}}
and the equality

g_{i}(w)=f(\zeta^{(i)}(w))-\langle\zeta^{(i)}(w),w\rangle

(6.31)

holds on
V
.

Proof.

By the condition

Z_{i}=p(\Lambda_{V,i})\subset U=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)

and (
6.26
),
the subset
\Lambda_{V,i}-\Lambda^{f}\subset(T^{*}U)_{\mathbb{R}}
of
(T^{*}U)_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq T^{*}U_{\mathbb{R}}
is
contained in the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-conic Lagrangian analytic
set
{\rm SS}(K)\cap(T^{*}U)_{\mathbb{R}}
.
By
[
24
, Lemma A.52]
this implies that
we have
\Lambda_{V,i}\subset T^{*}_{Z_{i}}X+\Lambda^{f}
.
Choose a holomorphic function
g_{i}
on
V\subset\Omega

such that
\chi(\Lambda_{V,i})=\Lambda^{g_{i}}
and set

k_{i}(w):=g_{i}(w)-f(\zeta^{(i)}(w))+\langle\zeta^{(i)}(w),w\rangle\qquad(w\in V).

(6.32)

Then by using the conditions
\zeta^{(i)}(w)\in Z_{i}

(
w\in V
) and
(\zeta^{(i)}(w),w)\in\Lambda_{V,i}\subset T^{*}_{Z_{i}}X+\Lambda^{f}

(
w\in V
)
we can easily show that the
condition (
6.30
) is equivalent to the one

\frac{\partial k_{i}}{\partial w_{j}}(w)=0\qquad(1\leq j\leq N,w\in V).

(6.33)

This implies that
k_{i}
is constant on
V
.
Then by substracting its value from
g_{i}

we obtain the assertion.
∎

Example 6.6
.

Consider the case where
N=2
and set
z=(z_{1},z_{2})=(x,y)
and

w=(w_{1},w_{2})=(\xi,\eta)
. Assume that

U=\{(x,y)\in X=\mathbb{C}^{2}\ |\ x\not=0\}\subset X
,

K=\mathbb{C}_{X}
and
the function
f:U\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
is defined to be
the ratio of
P(x,y)=y
and
Q(x,y)=x
as

f(x,y)=\frac{P(x,y)}{Q(x,y)}=\frac{y}{x}.

(6.34)

Then by (an analogue of) Lemma
5.3
we have

\Lambda={\rm SS}_{\rm irr}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})=\Lambda^{f}=\Bigl\{\bigl(x,y,-\frac{y}{x^{2}},\frac{1}{x}\bigr)\ |\ (x,y)\in U\Bigr\}\subset T^{*}X\simeq X\times Y.

(6.35)

By solving the equation
\xi=-\frac{y}{x^{2}}
,

\eta=\frac{1}{x}
, we find that
d=1
,

\Omega=\{(\xi,\eta)\in Y=\mathbb{C}^{2}\ |\ \eta\not=0\}\subset Y
and for

(\xi,\eta)\in\Omega
we have

x=\zeta_{1}(\xi,\eta):=\frac{1}{\eta},\quad y=\zeta_{2}(\xi,\eta):=-\frac{\xi}{\eta^{2}}.

(6.36)

We can also verify that the function

g(\xi,\eta):=f\bigl(\frac{1}{\eta},-\frac{\xi}{\eta^{2}}\bigr)-\Bigl\langle\bigl(\frac{1}{\eta},-\frac{\xi}{\eta^{2}}\bigr),(\xi,\eta)\Bigr\rangle=-\frac{\xi}{\eta}

(6.37)

satisfies the condition
\chi(\Lambda)=\Lambda^{g}
.

Example 6.7
.

Let
N=2
and
z=(z_{1},z_{2})=(x,y)
,

w=(w_{1},w_{2})=(\xi,\eta)
be as in Example
6.6
.
Assume that

U=\{(x,y)\in X=\mathbb{C}^{2}\ |\ x\not=0\}\subset X
,

K=\mathbb{C}_{X}
and
the function
f:U\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
is defined to be
the ratio of
P(x,y)=x-y^{3}
and
Q(x,y)=x
as

f(x,y)=\frac{P(x,y)}{Q(x,y)}=\frac{x-y^{3}}{x}=1-\frac{y^{3}}{x}.

(6.38)

Then we have

\Lambda_{0}:={\rm SS}_{\rm irr}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})\cap(T^{*}U)_{\mathbb{R}}=\Bigl\{\bigl(x,y,\frac{y^{3}}{x^{2}},-\frac{3y^{2}}{x}\bigr)\ |\ (x,y)\in U\Bigr\}\subset T^{*}X\simeq X\times Y.

(6.39)

Let us set

\Omega_{0}:=\{(\xi,\eta)\in Y=\mathbb{C}^{2}\ |\ \xi\not=0,\eta\not=0\}\quad\subset Y.

(6.40)

Then by solving the equation
\xi=\frac{y^{3}}{x^{2}}
,

\eta=-\frac{3y^{2}}{x}
, we find that for

(\xi,\eta)\in\Omega_{0}
we have

x=\zeta_{1}(\xi,\eta):=-\frac{\eta^{3}}{27\xi^{2}},\quad y=\zeta_{2}(\xi,\eta):=\frac{\eta^{2}}{9\xi}.

(6.41)

We can also verify that the function

g(\xi,\eta):=f\bigl(-\frac{\eta^{3}}{27\xi^{2}},\frac{\eta^{2}}{9\xi}\bigr)-\Bigl\langle\bigl(-\frac{\eta^{3}}{27\xi^{2}},\frac{\eta^{2}}{9\xi}\bigr),(\xi,\eta)\Bigr\rangle=1-\frac{\eta^{3}}{27\xi}

(6.42)

on
\Omega_{0}
satisfies the condition

\chi(\Lambda_{0})\cap q^{-1}(\Omega_{0})=\Lambda^{g}
.

Recall that for the closed embedding

\iota:(T^{*}X)\times\mathbb{C}\hookrightarrow T^{*}(X\times\mathbb{C})\qquad(((z,w),\tau)\longmapsto(z,\tau,w,1))

(6.43)

we have

{\rm SS}^{{\rm E},\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})=\iota^{-1}{\rm SS}(\mathcal{F}).

(6.44)

Then for
r+1\leq i\leq d
, by the proof of
Lemma
5.6
we obtain the following
description of
{\rm SS}^{{\rm E},\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})\cap(\Lambda_{V,i}\times\mathbb{C})
.
For
r+1\leq i\leq d
let

\rho_{i}:Z_{i}\times_{X}T^{*}X\longrightarrow T^{*}Z_{i}

be the natural morphism associated to the
inclusion map
Z_{i}\hookrightarrow X=\mathbb{C}^{N}
.

Lemma 6.8
.

For any
r+1\leq i\leq d
there exist holomorphic functions

h_{ik}:Z_{i}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}

(
1\leq k\leq n_{i}
) on
Z_{i}
and a
neighborhood
W
of
\Lambda_{V,i}
in
T^{*}X
such that for
the complex submanifolds

\Gamma_{k}:=\{(z,-h_{ik}(z))\in X\times\mathbb{C}\ |\ z\in Z_{i}\}\subset X\times\mathbb{C}\qquad(1\leq k\leq n_{i})

(6.45)

of
X\times\mathbb{C}
we have

{\rm SS}^{{\rm E},\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})=\bigcup_{k=1}^{n_{i}}\iota^{-1}\Bigl\{T^{*}_{\Gamma_{k}}(X\times\mathbb{C})\Bigr\}

(6.46)

in the open subset
W\times\mathbb{C}\subset(T^{*}X)\times\mathbb{C}

and

\rho_{i}(\Lambda_{V,i})=\Lambda^{h_{ik}}\qquad(1\leq k\leq n_{i}),

(6.47)

where we set

\Lambda^{h_{ik}}:=\{(z,dh_{ik}(z))\ |\ z\in Z_{i}\}\subset T^{*}Z_{i}.

(6.48)

Proof.

Note that as
\Lambda_{V,i}\simeq V

the complex manifold
Z_{i}=p(\Lambda_{V,i})\subset I(f)

is contractible. Let
\Lambda_{i}^{\circ}\subset q^{-1}(\Omega)\cap\Lambda=q^{-1}(\Omega)\cap{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})

be the (unique) connected component of

\Lambda^{\circ}:=q^{-1}(\Omega)\cap\Lambda
containing
the connected set
\Lambda_{V,i}\simeq V
.
Then by our choice of
\Omega\subset Y=\mathbb{C}^{N}
in Lemma
6.3
, the smooth variety

\Lambda_{i}^{\circ}
is a fiber bundle over
a smooth (quasi-affine) subvariety
S_{i}
of
X=\mathbb{C}^{N}

contained in
I(f)
such that
Z_{i}\subset S_{i}
. Note that even if
i\not=i^{\prime}

we may have
\Lambda_{i}^{\circ}=\Lambda_{i^{\prime}}^{\circ}
.
As the natural morphism

S_{i}\times_{X}T^{*}X\longrightarrow T^{*}S_{i}

associated to the
inclusion map
S_{i}\hookrightarrow X=\mathbb{C}^{N}

is an extension of
\rho_{i}
, we denote it
also by
\rho_{i}
. Note that the closure

\overline{\Lambda_{i}^{\circ}}
of
\Lambda_{i}^{\circ}

in
T^{*}X
is an irreducible component of
\Lambda
.
Moreover by the proof of
Lemma
5.6
,
there exists a “horizontal” stratum
M_{i}\subset X\times\mathbb{C}

over a Zariski open subset of
S_{i}

such that the closure of the smooth Lagrangian subvariety
of
T^{*}X
obtained by forgetting the homogeneity of
the conormal bundle
T^{*}_{M_{i}}(X\times\mathbb{C})\subset T^{*}(X\times\mathbb{C})
is equal to

\overline{\Lambda_{i}^{\circ}}
.
This implies that the subset

\overline{\Lambda_{i}^{\circ}}\cap(S_{i}\times_{X}T^{*}X)

of
S_{i}\times_{X}T^{*}X

is a union of some fibers of

\rho_{i}
. Namely there exists a Lagrangian
subvariety
\Lambda_{S_{i}}\subset T^{*}S_{i}

such that

\overline{\Lambda_{i}^{\circ}}\cap(S_{i}\times_{X}T^{*}X)=\rho_{i}^{-1}\Lambda_{S_{i}}.

(6.49)

Recall that
\Lambda_{i}^{\circ}
is smooth and a fiber bundle
over
S_{i}
. Since
\Lambda_{i}^{\circ}
is an open subset of

\rho_{i}^{-1}\Lambda_{S_{i}}
, for any point

(z_{0},w_{0})\in\Lambda_{i}^{\circ}
there exists
its neighborhood
W(z_{0},w_{0})
in
S_{i}\times_{X}T^{*}X

such that
\Lambda_{i}^{\circ}
is a union of
some fibers of
\rho_{i}
in
W(z_{0},w_{0})
. This implies
that the open subset
\Lambda_{S_{i}}^{\circ}:=\rho_{i}(\Lambda_{i}^{\circ})
of
\Lambda_{S_{i}}

is a complex Lagrangian submanifold of
T^{*}S_{i}
and
the morphism
\Lambda_{i}^{\circ}\longrightarrow\Lambda_{S_{i}}^{\circ}
induced by
\rho_{i}
is
a submersion. As the structure morphism

\Lambda_{i}^{\circ}\longrightarrow S_{i}
of
the fiber bundle
\Lambda_{i}^{\circ}
over
S_{i}

is the composite of the morphisms

\Lambda_{i}^{\circ}\longrightarrow\Lambda_{S_{i}}^{\circ}\longrightarrow S_{i},

(6.50)

the morphism
\Lambda_{S_{i}}^{\circ}\longrightarrow S_{i}
induced by
T^{*}S_{i}\longrightarrow S_{i}

is also a submersion. Moreover by the dimensional reason it is a
finite unramified covering. We denote its
degree by
e_{i}\geq 1
.
Restricting the covering
\Lambda_{S_{i}}^{\circ}\longrightarrow S_{i}

to the contractible open
subset
Z_{i}\subset S_{i}
, we obtain a trivial
covering
\Lambda_{Z_{i}}:=Z_{i}\times_{S_{i}}\Lambda_{S_{i}}^{\circ}\longrightarrow Z_{i}
of
Z_{i}
of degree
e_{i}
.
Since
\rho_{i}(\Lambda_{V,i})\subset\Lambda_{Z_{i}}
is connected,
it is contained in only one connected component
of
\Lambda_{Z_{i}}
.
Together with the condition
p(\Lambda_{V,i})=Z_{i}

we obtain
\rho_{i}(\Lambda_{V,i})\simeq Z_{i}
.
Then there exists a holomorphic function

h:Z_{i}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
on

Z_{i}
such that

\rho_{i}(\Lambda_{V,i})=\Lambda^{h}:=\{(z,dh(z))\ |\ z\in Z_{i}\}

(6.51)

in
T^{*}Z_{i}
. Such
h
is uniquely
determined up to constant
functions on
Z_{i}
. Since
\Lambda_{V,i}
is
contained in the smooth part of the Lagrangian
subvariety
\Lambda={\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})\subset T^{*}X
, we then
immediately obtain the assertion.
This completes the proof.
∎

Moreover by the condition (
6.47
)
we see also that for any

1\leq k,k^{\prime}\leq n_{i}

such that
k\not=k^{\prime}

we have
\Lambda_{h_{ik}}=\Lambda_{h_{ik^{\prime}}}
and hence

h_{ik}-h_{ik^{\prime}}:Z_{i}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}

is a non-zero constant function on
Z_{i}
.
Then as in the proof of Lemma
6.5

we obtain the following result.

Lemma 6.9
.

For any
r+1\leq i\leq d
and
1\leq k\leq n_{i}

there exists a unique
holomorphic function
g_{ik}
on
V\subset\Omega

such that
\chi(\Lambda_{V,i})=\Lambda^{g_{ik}}
and the equality

g_{ik}(w)=h_{ik}(\zeta^{(i)}(w))-\langle\zeta^{(i)}(w),w\rangle

(6.52)

holds on
V
.

Example 6.10
.

We consider the situation in Example
6.7
and
inherit the notations there.
Moreover we assume that
Z=X=\mathbb{C}^{2}
and
K=\mathbb{C}_{X}
.
For
w=(\xi,\eta)\in\Omega_{0}
the meromorphic
function
f^{w}:X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)=\mathbb{C}^{2}\setminus\{x=0\}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}

is written as

f^{w}(x,y)=(\xi x+\eta y)-f(x,y)=\frac{\xi x^{2}+\eta xy+y^{3}-x}{x}.

(6.53)

For
t\in\mathbb{C}
we set also

R_{t}^{w}(x,y):=(\xi x^{2}+\eta xy+y^{3}-x)-tx

(6.54)

so that we have

f^{w}(x,y)-t=\frac{R_{t}^{w}(x,y)}{Q(x,y)}\qquad(z=(x,y)\in U=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0))

(6.55)

and

\overline{(f^{w})^{-1}(t)}=(R_{t}^{w})^{-1}(0)\subset X=\mathbb{C}^{2}.

(6.56)

Then for
t\not=-1
the complex curve

(R_{t}^{w})^{-1}(0)
in
X=\mathbb{C}^{2}
is smooth
on a neighborhood of the origin

\{(0,0)\}=P^{-1}(0)\cap Q^{-1}(0)
.
If
t=-1
, for any
w=(\xi,\eta)\in\Omega_{0}

the defining polynomial

R_{-1}^{w}(x,y):=\xi x^{2}+\eta xy+y^{3}

(6.57)

of the curve
(R_{t}^{w})^{-1}(0)

is Newton non-degenerate at the origin

\{(0,0)\}
(see
[
66
, Section 3]

for the details).
Then by applying the
classical Kouchnirenko’s theorem
(see
[
38
]
) to it,
we see that for any
w=(\xi,\eta)\in\Omega

its Milnor number at the origin is equal to
1
.
Moreover in Example
2.12
we studied the
Milnor fiber of the rational function

f^{w}(x,y)-(-1)=\frac{R_{-1}^{w}(x,y)}{Q(x,y)}=\frac{\xi x^{2}+\eta xy+y^{3}}{x}\qquad(z=(x,y)\in U=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0))

(6.58)

precisely and obtained the isomorphisms

H^{j}\phi^{{\rm mero,c}}_{f^{w}-(-1)}(K[2])_{(0,0)}\simeq\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}&(j=-1)\\
\\
0&(\mbox{otherwise}).\end{cases}

(6.59)

By Proposition
5.7
this implies that
for the conormal bundle
T^{*}_{\{(0,0)\}}X(\simeq\{(0,0)\}\times Y)\subset T^{*}X(\simeq X\times Y)
of
the complex submanifold
\{(0,0)\}\subset X
the subset
(T^{*}_{\{(0,0)\}}X)\times\{-1\}\subset(T^{*}X)\times\mathbb{C}
is
contained in
{\rm SS}^{{\rm E},\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})
.
We thus obtain

{\rm SS}^{\mathbb{C}}_{{\rm irr}}(\mathcal{F})=\Bigl(T^{*}_{\{(0,0)\}}X\Bigr)\cup\Lambda_{0}

(6.60)

(for the definition of
\Lambda_{0}
see
Example
6.7
). In particular,
we can take
\Omega
to be
\Omega_{0}

in this case.

For a point
w\in V\subset\Omega
we define
two regular functions
\ell^{w}:X=\mathbb{C}^{N}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
and

f^{w}:U=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
by

\ell^{w}:X\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}\qquad(z\longmapsto\langle z,w\rangle)

(6.61)

and

f^{w}=\ell^{w}|_{U}-f:U=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}\qquad(z\longmapsto\langle z,w\rangle-f(z))

(6.62)

respectively.

Lemma 6.11
.

For any
1\leq i\leq r
and

w\in V\subset\Omega
the holomorphic function

f^{w}|_{Z_{i}}:Z_{i}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
on
Z_{i}

has a non-degenerate (complex Morse)
critical point at
\zeta^{(i)}(w)\in Z_{i}
.

Proof.

In the proof of Lemma
6.5
,
for
1\leq i\leq r
we saw that

\Lambda_{V,i}
is an open subset of

T^{*}_{Z_{i}}X+\Lambda^{f}\subset T^{*}U
.
In other words, for the diffeomorphism

\Phi:T^{*}U\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}T^{*}U\qquad((z,w)\longmapsto(z,w+df(z))

(6.63)

of
T^{*}U
we have the inclusion

\Lambda_{V,i}\subset\Phi(T^{*}_{Z_{i}}X).

(6.64)

Moreover, for
w\in V
the condition

(\zeta^{(i)}(w),w)\in\Lambda_{V,i}
implies

(\zeta^{(i)}(w),df^{w}(\zeta^{(i)}(w)))=(\zeta^{(i)}(w),w-df(\zeta^{(i)}(w)))\in T^{*}_{Z_{i}}X

(6.65)

and hence the holomorphic function

f^{w}|_{Z_{i}}:Z_{i}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}

has a critical point at

\zeta^{(i)}(w)\in Z_{i}
.
By the choice of
\Omega
, for any
w\in V\subset\Omega
the fiber

q^{-1}(w)\simeq X=\mathbb{C}^{N}
of
q:X\times Y\longrightarrow Y
intersects
\Lambda_{V,i}\subset\Phi(T^{*}_{Z_{i}}X)
transversally.
Moreover, we have
q^{-1}(w)=\Lambda^{\ell^{w}}
.
This implies that
\Phi^{-1}(\Lambda^{\ell^{w}})=\Lambda^{\ell^{w}-f}=\Lambda^{f^{w}}
intersects

\Phi^{-1}(\Lambda_{V,i})\subset T^{*}_{Z_{i}}X

transversally. Then by Kashiwara-Schapira

[
26
, Lemma 7.2.2]
, the holomorphic function

f^{w}|_{Z_{i}}=(\ell^{w}-f)|_{Z_{i}}:Z_{i}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
on
Z_{i}

has a non-degenerate (complex Morse)
critical point at

\zeta^{(i)}(w)\in Z_{i}
.
∎

As in the proof of Lemma
6.11
,
by Lemma
6.8

we obtain also the following result.
For
r+1\leq i\leq d
,
1\leq k\leq n_{i}
and

w\in V\subset\Omega
we
define a holomorphic function

h_{ik}^{w}:Z_{i}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
on
Z_{i}
by

h_{ik}^{w}:=\ell^{w}|_{Z_{i}}-h_{ik}:Z_{i}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C},\qquad(z\longmapsto\langle z,w\rangle-h_{ik}(z))

(6.66)

so that we have

g_{ik}(w)=h_{ik}(\zeta^{(i)}(w))-\langle\zeta^{(i)}(w),w\rangle=-h_{ik}^{w}(\zeta^{(i)}(w))\qquad(w\in V).

(6.67)

Lemma 6.12
.

For any
r+1\leq i\leq d
,
1\leq k\leq n_{i}
and

w\in V\subset\Omega
the function

h_{ik}^{w}:Z_{i}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
on
Z_{i}

has a non-degenerate (complex Morse)
critical point at
\zeta^{(i)}(w)\in Z_{i}
.

Note that
for
1\leq i\leq r
and
w\in V\subset\Omega

the point

(\zeta^{(i)}(w),df^{w}(\zeta^{(i)}(w)))=(\zeta^{(i)}(w),w-df(\zeta^{(i)}(w)))\in(T^{*}X)_{\mathbb{R}}

(6.68)

is contained in the intersection of

T^{*}_{Z_{i}}X
and
the smooth part of
{\rm SS}(K|_{U})\subset(T^{*}U)_{\mathbb{R}}
.
We denote by
m(i)\geq 1
the multiplicity of
the regular holonomic

\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{N}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize rh}}(\mathcal{D}_{X})
(or
of the perverse sheaf
K[N]
) there.

Proposition 6.13
.

For any
1\leq i\leq r
and
w\in V\subset\Omega

we have isomorphisms

H^{j}\phi^{{\rm mero,c}}_{f^{w}-c}(K[N])_{\zeta^{(i)}(w)}\simeq H^{j}\phi_{f^{w}-c}(K[N])_{\zeta^{(i)}(w)}\simeq\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}^{m(i)}&(j=-1)\\
\\
0&(\mbox{otherwise}),\end{cases}

(6.69)

where we set
c:=f^{w}(\zeta^{(i)}(w))=\langle\zeta^{(i)}(w),w\rangle-f(\zeta^{(i)}(w))\in\mathbb{C}
.

Proof.

Since for
1\leq i\leq r
we have

\zeta^{(i)}(w)\in Z_{i}\subset U=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)
and hence
the function
f^{w}-c
is holomorphic
on a neighborhood of
\zeta^{(i)}(w)
,
there exists an isomorphism

\phi^{{\rm mero,c}}_{f^{w}-c}(K[N])_{\zeta^{(i)}(w)}\simeq\phi_{f^{w}-c}(K[N])_{\zeta^{(i)}(w)}.

(6.70)

Moreover by our definition of

m(i)\geq 1
the perverse sheaf
K[N]\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X^{{\rm an}})

is isomorphic to
\mathbb{C}_{Z_{i}}^{\oplus m(i)}[{\rm dim}Z_{i}]\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X^{{\rm an}})
in the
localized category

{\bf D}^{\mathrm{b}}(X^{{\rm an}},\{(\zeta^{(i)}(w),df^{w}(\zeta^{(i)}(w)))\})

at the point
(\zeta^{(i)}(w),df^{w}(\zeta^{(i)}(w)))\in T^{*}X^{{\rm an}}

(see
[
27
, Definition 6.1.1]

for the definition). Then by

[
27
, Proposition 8.6.3]

we obtain isomorphisms

\displaystyle\phi_{f^{w}-c}(K[N])_{\zeta^{(i)}(w)}

\displaystyle\simeq\phi_{f^{w}-f^{w}(\zeta^{(i)}(w))}(\mathbb{C}_{Z_{i}}^{\oplus m(i)}[{\rm dim}Z_{i}])_{\zeta^{(i)}(w)}

\displaystyle\simeq\phi_{f^{w}|_{Z_{i}}-f^{w}(\zeta^{(i)}(w))}(\mathbb{C}_{Z_{i}}^{\oplus m(i)}[{\rm dim}Z_{i}])_{\zeta^{(i)}(w)}.

Recall now that by Lemma
6.11
the holomorphic function

f^{w}|_{Z_{i}}:Z_{i}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
on
Z_{i}

has a non-degenerate (complex Morse)
critical point at
\zeta^{(i)}(w)\in Z_{i}
.
Then the Milnor fiber at it is
equal to one and hence we obtain the desired
isomorphisms

\displaystyle H^{j}\phi_{f^{w}-c}(K[N])_{\zeta^{(i)}(w)}

\displaystyle\simeq H^{j}\phi_{f^{w}|_{Z_{i}}-f^{w}(\zeta^{(i)}(w))}(\mathbb{C}_{Z_{i}}^{\oplus m(i)}[{\rm dim}Z_{i}])_{\zeta^{(i)}(w)}

\displaystyle\simeq\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}^{m(i)}&(j=-1)\\
\\
0&(\mbox{otherwise})\end{cases}

(see e.g.

[
66
, Theorem 2.6]
).
∎

Note that by (
6.46
)
for
r+1\leq i\leq d
,
1\leq k\leq n_{i}

and
w\in V\subset\Omega

the point

(\zeta^{(i)}(w),-h_{ik}(\zeta^{(i)}(w)),w,1)\in T^{*}(X\times\mathbb{C})

(6.71)

is contained in the smooth part of

{\rm SS}(\mathcal{F}[N])={\rm SS}(\mathcal{F})\subset T^{*}(X\times\mathbb{C})
.
We denote by
m(i,k)\geq 1
the multiplicity of
of the perverse sheaf

\mathcal{F}[N]=(i_{-f})_{!}(K[N]|_{U})\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X^{{\rm an}}\times\mathbb{C})
there.

Proposition 6.14
.

For any
r+1\leq i\leq d
,
1\leq k\leq n_{i}

and
w\in V\subset\Omega

we have isomorphisms

H^{j}\phi^{{\rm mero,c}}_{f^{w}-c_{k}}(K[N])_{\zeta^{(i)}(w)}\simeq\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}^{m(i,k)}&(j=-1)\\
\\
0&(\mbox{otherwise}),\end{cases}

(6.72)

where we set
c_{k}:=h_{ik}^{w}(\zeta^{(i)}(w))=\langle\zeta^{(i)}(w),w\rangle-h_{ik}(\zeta^{(i)}(w))\in\mathbb{C}
. Moreover,
for any complex number

c\in\mathbb{C}
satisfying the condition
c\not=c_{k}

(
1\leq k\leq n_{i}
) we have a vanishing

\phi^{{\rm mero,c}}_{f^{w}-c}(K[N])_{\zeta^{(i)}(w)}\simeq 0.

(6.73)

Proof.

Note that for the (not necessarily closed) embedding

i_{f^{w}}:U\hookrightarrow X\times\mathbb{C}

(
z\longmapsto(z,f^{w}(z))
) and any
c\in\mathbb{C}

we have an isomorphism

\phi^{{\rm mero,c}}_{f^{w}-c}(K[N])_{\zeta^{(i)}(w)}\simeq\phi_{\tau-c}\bigl((i_{f^{w}})_{!}(K[N]|_{U})\bigr)_{(\zeta^{(i)}(w),\ c)}.

(6.74)

As in the proof of Proposition
5.7
,
let us consider the
automorphism
T_{w}
of
X\times\mathbb{C}
defined by

T_{w}:X\times\mathbb{C}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}X\times\mathbb{C},\qquad((z,\tau)\longmapsto(z,\tau+\langle z,w\rangle)).

(6.75)

Then we have
i_{f^{w}}=T_{w}\circ i_{-f}
and
hence there exists an isomorphism

(T_{w})_{*}(\mathcal{F}[N])=(T_{w})_{*}(i_{-f})_{!}(K[N]|_{U})\simeq(i_{f^{w}})_{!}(K[N]|_{U}).

(6.76)

On the other hand, by (
6.46
)
and our definition of
m(i,k)\geq 1
the perverse
sheaf
\mathcal{F}[N]\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X^{{\rm an}}\times\mathbb{C})

is isomorphic to
\mathbb{C}_{\Gamma_{k}}^{\oplus m(i,k)}[{\rm dim}\Gamma_{k}]\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X^{{\rm an}}\times\mathbb{C})
in the
localized category

{\bf D}^{\mathrm{b}}(X^{{\rm an}}\times\mathbb{C},\{(\zeta^{(i)}(w),-h_{ik}(\zeta^{(i)}(w)),w,1)\})

at the point
(\zeta^{(i)}(w),-h_{ik}(\zeta^{(i)}(w)),w,1)\in T^{*}(X^{{\rm an}}\times\mathbb{C})

(see
[
27
, Definition 6.1.1]
). This implies
that for the closed embedding

i_{k}:Z_{i}\hookrightarrow X\times\mathbb{C},\qquad(z\longmapsto(z,h_{ik}^{w}(z)))

(6.77)

we have an isomorphism

(i_{f^{w}})_{!}(K[N]|_{U})\simeq(i_{k})_{*}(\mathbb{C}_{Z_{i}}^{\oplus m(i,k)}[{\rm dim}Z_{i}])

(6.78)

in the localized category

{\bf D}^{\mathrm{b}}(X^{{\rm an}}\times\mathbb{C},\{(\zeta^{(i)}(w),c_{k},0,1)\})

at the point
(\zeta^{(i)}(w),c_{k},0,1)\in T^{*}(X^{{\rm an}}\times\mathbb{C})
. Then by

[
27
, Proposition 8.6.3]

we obtain isomorphisms

\displaystyle\phi_{\tau-c_{k}}\bigl((i_{f^{w}})_{!}(K[N]|_{U})\bigr)_{(\zeta^{(i)}(w),\ c_{k})}

\displaystyle\simeq\phi_{\tau-h_{ik}^{w}(\zeta^{(i)}(w))}\bigl((i_{k})_{*}(\mathbb{C}_{Z_{i}}^{\oplus m(i,k)}[{\rm dim}Z_{i}])\bigr)_{(\zeta^{(i)}(w),\ h_{ik}^{w}(\zeta^{(i)}(w)))}

\displaystyle\simeq\phi_{h_{ik}^{w}-h_{ik}^{w}(\zeta^{(i)}(w))}\bigl(\mathbb{C}_{Z_{i}}^{\oplus m(i,k)}[{\rm dim}Z_{i}]\bigr)_{\zeta^{(i)}(w)}.

Recall that by Lemma
6.12
the holomorphic function

h_{ik}^{w}|_{Z_{i}}:Z_{i}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
on
Z_{i}

has a non-degenerate (complex Morse)
critical point at
\zeta^{(i)}(w)\in Z_{i}
.
Then the first assertion immediately follows
from the standard fact that the Milnor number at it
is equal to one (see e.g.

[
66
, Theorem 2.6]
). Similarly,
we can show the second assertion.
∎

By the proof of Proposition
6.14
and
(
6.46
), for any

r+1\leq i\leq d
,

w\in V\subset\Omega
and

c\in\mathbb{C}

such that
c\not=h_{ik}^{w}(\zeta^{(i)}(w))

(
1\leq k\leq n_{i}
) we have also a vanishing

\phi^{{\rm mero,c}}_{f^{w}-c}(K[N])_{\zeta^{(i)}(w)}\simeq 0.

(6.79)

Together with Proposition
6.14

this implies that for any
r+1\leq i\leq d

the positive integer

m(i):=\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n_{i}}m(i,k)\geq 1

(6.80)

satisfies the condition

m(i)=\displaystyle\sum_{c\in\mathbb{C}}{\rm dim}H^{N-1}\phi^{{\rm mero,c}}_{f^{w}-c}(K)_{\zeta^{(i)}(w)}\qquad(w\in V).

(6.81)

Corollary 6.15
.

For the open subset
q^{-1}(\Omega)=X\times\Omega\subset X\times Y
of

X\times Y\simeq T^{*}X
we have

{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})\cap q^{-1}(\Omega)={\rm SS}_{{\rm eva}}(F)\cap q^{-1}(\Omega).

(6.82)

Proof.

By Proposition
5.7
it suffices
to prove only the inclusion

\Lambda\cap q^{-1}(\Omega)={\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{F})\cap q^{-1}(\Omega)\subset{\rm SS}_{{\rm eva}}(F)\cap q^{-1}(\Omega).

(6.83)

For a point
(z_{0},w_{0})\in\Lambda\cap q^{-1}(\Omega)

we take a contractible open subset

V\subset\Omega
such that
q((z_{0},w_{0}))=w_{0}\in V

and use the several notations that we introduced
for it above. Then by Propositions
6.13

and
6.14

we obtain the desired condition

(z_{0},w_{0})\in{\rm SS}_{{\rm eva}}(F)\cap q^{-1}(\Omega)
.
∎

Lemma 6.16
.

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a Whitney stratification of
Z\cap U\subset U=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)
adapted to

K|_{U}\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(U)
such that

{\rm SS}(K|_{U})\subset\bigcup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}T^{*}_{S}X

(6.84)

and
\mathcal{S}_{{\rm red}}\subset\mathcal{S}
its subset consisting of
strata
S\in\mathcal{S}
satisfying the condition

T^{*}_{S}X\subset{\rm SS}(K|_{U})
so that we have

{\rm SS}(K|_{U})=\bigcup_{S\in\mathcal{S}_{{\rm red}}}\overline{T^{*}_{S}X}.

(6.85)

Then for any
w\in V\subset\Omega

and
S\in\mathcal{S}_{{\rm red}}
the
holomorphic function
f^{w}|_{S}:S\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
on
S
is tame at infinity.

Proof.

Since by definition the morphisms
\zeta^{(i)}:V\longrightarrow U=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)
(
1\leq i\leq r
) are holomorphic,
there exist
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
and
R\gg 0

such that for the open ball
B_{\varepsilon}(w)\subset V\subset\Omega
(resp.
B_{R}(0)\subset X=\mathbb{C}^{N}
) with radius

\varepsilon>0
(resp.
R>0
)
centered at
w\in V
(resp. the origin
0\in X=\mathbb{C}^{N}
)
we have the inclusion

\zeta^{(i)}(B_{\varepsilon}(w))\subset U\cap B_{R}(0)\qquad(1\leq i\leq r).

(6.86)

In other words, the subset

\Lambda\cap p^{-1}(U)\cap q^{-1}(B_{\varepsilon}(w))=\Bigl\{{\rm SS}(K|_{U})+\Lambda^{f}\Bigr\}\cap q^{-1}(B_{\varepsilon}(w))

(6.87)

of
p^{-1}(U)\cap q^{-1}(B_{\varepsilon}(w))
is contained in

p^{-1}(U\cap B_{R}(0))\cap q^{-1}(B_{\varepsilon}(w))
.
This implies that for the stratum
S\in\mathcal{S}_{{\rm red}}
and
any
u\in B_{\varepsilon}(0)\subset Y=\mathbb{C}^{N}
the linear perturbation of
f^{w}|_{S}
:

(f^{w+u}|_{S})(z)=(f^{w}|_{S})(z)+\langle z,u\rangle\qquad(z\in S)

(6.88)

has no critical point in the set

S\setminus B_{R}(0)=\{z\in S\ |\ ||z||\geq R\}.

(6.89)

Suppose now that there exists a point
z\in S\setminus B_{R}(0)

such that
||d{\rm Re}(f^{w}|_{S})(z)||<\varepsilon
.
Then, the image of
B_{\varepsilon}(0)\subset\mathbb{C}^{N}
by the surjective linear map

\Psi_{z}:T^{*}_{z}X_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq\mathbb{C}^{N}\longrightarrow T^{*}_{z}S_{\mathbb{R}}
being also an open ball with
radius
\varepsilon>0
, we have the
equality
d{\rm Re}(f^{w}|_{S})(z)=\Psi_{z}(u)
for some
u\in B_{\varepsilon}(0)
.
Moreover, for the holomorphic function

\ell^{\overline{u}}:S\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
on
S

defined by

\ell^{\overline{u}}(z):=\langle z,\overline{u}\rangle=(z,u)\qquad(z\in S)

(6.90)

we have
d{\rm Re}\ell^{\overline{u}}(z)=\Psi_{z}(u)
by (
3.22
).
This implies that the holomorphic
function
(f^{w-\overline{u}}|_{S})(z)
on
S
satisfies
the condition
d{\rm Re}(f^{w-\overline{u}}|_{S})(z)=0

at the point
z\in S\setminus B_{R}(0)
.
Then by the Cauchy-Riemann equation, we obtain
also
d(f^{w-\overline{u}}|_{S})(z)=0
.
Since we have
-\overline{u}\in B_{\varepsilon}(0)
,
we get a contradiction.
∎

Similarly, by the proofs of Lemma
6.8

and Proposition
6.14

we obtain the following result. For
a point
w\in\Omega
by the (not necessarily
closed) embedding
i_{f^{w}}:U\hookrightarrow X\times\mathbb{C}
(
z\longmapsto(z,f^{w}(z))
) we set

L^{w}:=(i_{f^{w}})_{!}(K|_{U})\quad\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X\times\mathbb{C}).

(6.91)

Lemma 6.17
.

For a point
w\in V\subset\Omega
let
\mathcal{S}
and
\mathcal{S}_{0}

be Whitney stratifications of

X\times\mathbb{C}
and
X
respectively such that

{\rm SS}(L^{w})\subset\bigcup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}T^{*}_{S}(X\times\mathbb{C})

(6.92)

and the projection
X\times\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow X
is a stratified fiber bundle as in
the assertion of the theorem in

[
15
, page 43]
. Then for any stratum
S\in\mathcal{S}

in
\mathcal{S}
such that
T^{*}_{S}(X\times\mathbb{C})\subset{\rm SS}(L^{w})

the restriction
h|_{S}:S\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}

of the function
h:X\times\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}

(
(z,\tau)\longmapsto\tau
) to
S\subset X\times\mathbb{C}

is relatively tame at infinity for the projection

X\times\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow X
in the sense of Definition
3.9
.

With Theorem
3.7
, Lemmas
6.5
,

6.11
and
6.16

and Proposition
6.13

at hands,
in the special case where
I(f)=P^{-1}(0)\cap Q^{-1}(0)=\emptyset
(
\Longrightarrow r=d
) we obtain the following
result as in the proof of
[
22
, Theorem 4.4]

(see the proof of Theorem
6.19
below for the details).

Theorem 6.18
.

In the situation as above, assume also that

I(f)=P^{-1}(0)\cap Q^{-1}(0)=\emptyset
so that we have
r=d
.
Then we have an isomorphism

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes\big(Sol_{\overline{Y}}^{\rm E}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}^{\wedge}})\big)\simeq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{d}\bigl(\mathbb{E}_{V^{{\rm an}}|\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}g_{i}}\bigr)^{\oplus m(i)}

(6.93)

of enhanced ind-sheaves on
\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}
.

In the general case i.e. if we do not
assume the condition
I(f)=\emptyset
,
we have the following result.
Note that by Lemma
6.9

for any
r+1\leq i\leq d
and

1\leq k,k^{\prime}\leq n_{i}

such that
k\not=k^{\prime}
the difference

g_{ik}-g_{ik^{\prime}}:Z_{i}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}

is a non-zero constant function on
Z_{i}
.
Then for any

r+1\leq i\leq d
and
1\leq k,k^{\prime}\leq n_{i}

we have an isomorphism

\mathbb{E}_{V^{{\rm an}}|\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}g_{ik}}\simeq\mathbb{E}_{V^{{\rm an}}|\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}g_{ik^{\prime}}}

(6.94)

of enhanced ind-sheaves on
\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}
.
For this reason, for each
r+1\leq i\leq d
by
choosing an index
1\leq k\leq n_{i}
we
set
g_{i}(w):=g_{ik}(w)
(
w\in V
)
and consider the enhanced ind-sheaf

\mathbb{E}_{V^{{\rm an}}|\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}g_{i}}

in what follows.

Theorem 6.19
.

In the situation as above, we have an isomorphism

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes\big(Sol_{\overline{Y}}^{\rm E}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}^{\wedge}})\big)\simeq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{d}\bigl(\mathbb{E}_{V^{{\rm an}}|\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}g_{i}}\bigr)^{\oplus m(i)}

(6.95)

of enhanced ind-sheaves on
\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}
.

Proof.

By (
6.20
) and (
6.94
),
it suffices to prove that there exists an isomorphism

\displaystyle\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes{}^{\rm L}\Bigl\{\bigl\{\pi^{-1}(i_{X^{{\rm an}}})_{!}K\bigr\}\otimes{\rm E}_{U^{{\rm an}}|\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}f}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\simeq\Bigl(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\bigl({\rm E}_{V^{{\rm an}}|\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}g_{i}}\bigr)^{\oplus m(i)}\Bigr)\bigoplus\Bigl\{\bigoplus_{i=r+1}^{d}\Bigl(\bigoplus_{k=1}^{n_{i}}\bigl({\rm E}_{V^{{\rm an}}|\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}g_{ik}}\bigr)^{\oplus m(i,k)}\Bigr)\Bigr\}.

of enhanced sheaves on
V\subset\Omega\subset Y
.
Let

X\times\mathbb{R}_{s}\overset{p_{1}}{\longleftarrow}(X\times\mathbb{R}_{s})\times(Y\times\mathbb{R}_{t})\overset{p_{2}}{\longrightarrow}Y\times\mathbb{R}_{t}

(6.96)

be the projections.
Then by D’Agnolo-Kashiwara
[
10
, Lemma 7.2.1]

on
Y^{{\rm an}}\subset\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}
we have an isomorphism

\displaystyle{}^{\rm L}\Bigl\{\bigl\{\pi^{-1}(i_{X^{{\rm an}}})_{!}K\bigr\}\otimes{\rm E}_{U^{{\rm an}}|\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}f}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbf{Q}\Bigl({\rm R}p_{2!}\Bigl(p_{1}^{-1}\Bigl\{\bigl(\pi^{-1}K\bigr)\otimes{\rm E}_{U^{{\rm an}}|X^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}f}\Bigr\}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\{t-s-{\rm Re}\langle z,w\rangle\geq 0\}}[N]\Bigl)\Bigr),

where
\mathbf{Q}:{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{Y^{{\rm an}}\times\mathbb{R}})\to{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{Y^{{\rm an}}})
is the quotient functor.
For a point
(w,t)\in Y^{{\rm an}}\times\mathbb{R}

we have also isomorphisms

\displaystyle\Bigl({\rm R}p_{2!}\Bigl(p_{1}^{-1}\Bigl\{\bigl(\pi^{-1}K\bigr)\otimes{\rm E}_{U^{{\rm an}}|X^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}f}\Bigr\}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\{t-s-{\rm Re}\langle z,w\rangle\geq 0\}}[N]\Bigl)\Bigr)_{(w,t)}

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}(\{(z,s)\in U^{{\rm an}}\times\mathbb{R}\ |\ t-s-{\rm Re}\langle z,w\rangle\geq 0,s+{\rm Re}f(z)\geq 0\};\pi^{-1}K[N])

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}(U^{{\rm an}};{\rm R}\pi_{!}(\mathbb{C}_{\{(z,s)\in U^{{\rm an}}\times\mathbb{R}\ |\ t-s-{\rm Re}\langle z,w\rangle\geq 0,\ s+{\rm Re}f(z)\geq 0\}}\otimes\pi^{-1}K[N]))

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}(U^{{\rm an}};({\rm R}\pi_{!}\mathbb{C}_{\{(z,s)\in U^{{\rm an}}\times\mathbb{R}\ |\ t-s-{\rm Re}\langle z,w\rangle\geq 0,\ s+{\rm Re}f(z)\geq 0\}})\otimes K[N])

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}(\{z\in U^{{\rm an}}\ |\ {\rm Re}f^{w}(z)\leq t\};K[N]),

where we used

{\rm R}\pi_{!}\mathbb{C}_{\{(z,s)\in U^{{\rm an}}\times\mathbb{R}\ |\ t-s-{\rm Re}\langle z,w\rangle\geq 0,\ s+{\rm Re}f(z)\geq 0\}}\simeq\mathbb{C}_{\{z\in U^{{\rm an}}\ |\ {\rm Re}\langle z,w\rangle-{\rm Re}f(z)\leq t\}}

(6.97)

in the last isomorphism.
Fix a point
w\in V\subset\Omega\subset Y=\mathbb{C}^{N}
.
Then as in the proof
of
[
22
, Theorem 4.4]

we can prove the vanishing

{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}(\{z\in U^{{\rm an}}\ |\ {\rm Re}f^{w}(z)\leq t\};K[N])\simeq 0

(6.98)

for
t\ll 0
as follows. Let us set

\displaystyle L(Sol_{X}(\mathcal{M})):=

\displaystyle\Bigl({\rm R}p_{2!}\Bigl(p_{1}^{-1}\Bigl\{\bigl(\pi^{-1}K\bigr)\otimes{\rm E}_{U^{{\rm an}}|X^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}f}\Bigr\}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\{t-s-{\rm Re}\langle z,w\rangle\geq 0\}}[N]\Bigl)\Bigr).

Then for
t\in\mathbb{R}
its stalk
at
(w,t)\in Y\times\mathbb{R}
:

\big(L(Sol_{X}(\mathcal{M}))\big)_{(w,t)}\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}\big(\{z\in U^{{\rm an}}\ |\ {\rm Re}f^{w}\leq t\};K[N]\big)

(6.99)

is calculated as follows:

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}\big(\{z\in U^{{\rm an}}\ |\ {\rm Re}f^{w}\leq t\};K[N]\big)

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}\big(\{\tau\in\mathbb{C}\ |\ {\rm Re}\tau\leq t\};{\rm R}f^{w}_{!}(K|_{U}[N])\big).

Since the morphism
f^{w}:U=X\setminus Q^{-1}(0)\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
is algebraic,
the proper direct image
{\rm R}f^{w}_{!}(K|_{U}[N])

of the perverse sheaf

(K|_{U}[N])\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(U^{{\rm an}})

is constructible. Then in particular,
for
t\ll 0
the restrictions of
its cohomology sheaves to
the closed half space
