\{\tau\in\mathbb{C}\ |\ {\rm Re}\tau\leq t\}\subset\mathbb{C}
of
\mathbb{C}
are
locally constant.
Thus for
t\ll 0
we obtain the vanishing

{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}\bigl(\{\tau\in\mathbb{C}\ |\ {\rm Re}\tau\leq t\};{\rm R}f^{w}_{!}(K|_{U}[N])\bigr)\simeq 0.

(6.100)

For
1\leq i\leq r
we set

c(i):=f^{w}(\zeta^{(i)}(w))=\langle\zeta^{(i)}(w),w\rangle-f(\zeta^{(i)}(w))\in\mathbb{C}.

(6.101)

Moreover for
r+1\leq i\leq d
and

1\leq k\leq n_{i}
we set

c(i,k):=h_{ik}^{w}(\zeta^{(i)}(w))=\langle\zeta^{(i)}(w),w\rangle-h_{ik}(\zeta^{(i)}(w))\in\mathbb{C}.

(6.102)

Let
B\subset\mathbb{C}
be the union
of these points in
\mathbb{C}
and write it as

B=\{c_{1},c_{2},\ldots,c_{l}\}=\{c_{j}\in\mathbb{C}\ |\ 1\leq j\leq l\}\subset\mathbb{C}

(6.103)

so that for any
j\not=j^{\prime}

we have
c_{j}\not=c_{j^{\prime}}
.
Then for any
1\leq i\leq r
there
exists a unique index
1\leq j\leq l

such that
c_{j}=c(i)
and we denote it by

j(i)
. Similarly, for any
r+1\leq i\leq d
and

1\leq k\leq n_{i}
there exists a unique index
1\leq j\leq l

such that
c_{j}=c(i,k)
and we denote it by

j(i,k)
. Then by
Corollary
6.15
and Lemma
6.17
,
for any
1\leq j\leq l
we can apply
Theorem
3.11

to obtain an isomorphism

\displaystyle\phi_{\tau-c_{j}}({\rm R}f^{w}_{!}(K|_{U}[N]))

\displaystyle\simeq\Bigl(\bigoplus_{i:\ j(i)=j}\phi_{f^{w}-c_{j}}(K[N])_{\zeta^{(i)}(w)}\Bigr)\bigoplus\Bigl(\bigoplus_{(i,k):\ j(i,k)=j}\phi^{{\rm mero,c}}_{f^{w}-c_{j}}(K[N])_{\zeta^{(i)}(w)}\Bigr),

where in the first (resp. the last) direct sum

\oplus
the index
1\leq i\leq r
(resp.
the pair
(i,k)
of
r+1\leq i\leq d
and

1\leq k\leq n_{i}
) ranges over the ones
satisfying the condition
j(i)=j
(resp.

j(i,k)=j
). Recall that for any
r+1\leq i\leq d

there exists at most one index
1\leq k\leq n_{i}

such that
j(i,k)=j
.
By Proposition
6.13

for any
1\leq i\leq r

such that
j(i)=j

we have isomorphisms

H^{n}\phi_{f^{w}-c_{j}}(K[N])_{\zeta^{(i)}(w)}\simeq\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}^{m(i)}&(n=-1)\\
\\
0&(\mbox{otherwise}).\end{cases}

(6.104)

Moreover by Proposition
6.14

for any pair
(i,k)
of
r+1\leq i\leq d
and

1\leq k\leq n_{i}

such that
j(i,k)=j

we have isomorphisms

H^{n}\phi^{{\rm mero,c}}_{f^{w}-c_{j}}(K[N])_{\zeta^{(i)}(w)}\simeq\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}^{m(i,k)}&(n=-1)\\
\\
0&(\mbox{otherwise}).\end{cases}

(6.105)

For
1\leq j\leq l
let us set

d_{j}:=\Bigl(\displaystyle\sum_{i:\ j(i)=j}m(i)\Bigr)+\Bigl(\displaystyle\sum_{(i,k):\ j(i,k)=j}m(i,k)\Bigr).

(6.106)

Then for any
1\leq j\leq l
we thus obtain isomorphisms

H^{n}\phi_{\tau-c_{j}}({\rm R}f^{w}_{!}(K|_{U}[N]))\simeq\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}^{d_{j}}&(n=-1)\\
\\
0&(\mbox{otherwise}).\end{cases}

(6.107)

Again by Theorem
3.11
, for any
c\in\mathbb{C}

such that
c\notin B=\{c_{1},c_{2},\ldots,c_{l}\}

we can apply Proposition
6.14
to
obtain a vanishing

\phi_{\tau-c}({\rm R}f^{w}_{!}(K|_{U}[N]))\simeq 0.

(6.108)

By the proof of
[
23
, Lemma 2.1]

this implies that
the constructible sheaf
{\rm R}f^{w}_{!}(K|_{U}[N])\in{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathbb{C}-c}(\mathbb{C})
on
\mathbb{C}

is smooth outside the finite subset

B=\{c_{1},c_{2},\ldots,c_{l}\}\subset\mathbb{C}
.
For
1\leq j\leq l
set
\gamma_{j}:={\rm Re}c_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
.
For the fixed point
w\in V\subset\Omega
,
after reordering
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{l}\in\mathbb{R}

we may assume that

\gamma_{1}\ \leq\gamma_{2}\ \leq\cdots\cdots\ \leq\gamma_{l}.

If for some
1\leq i<j\leq l
such that

\gamma_{i}<\gamma_{j}

the open interval
(\gamma_{i},\gamma_{j})\subset\mathbb{R}

does not intersect the set
\{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{l}\}
,
then for any
t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\gamma_{i}<t_{1}<t_{2}<\gamma_{j}

we can show an isomorphism

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}(\{z\in U^{{\rm an}}\ |\ {\rm Re}f^{w}(z)\leq t_{2}\};K[N])

(6.109)

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}(\{z\in U^{{\rm an}}\ |\ {\rm Re}f^{w}(z)\leq t_{1}\};K[N]).

(6.110)

Equivalently, we shall show an isomorphism

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}(\{\tau\in\mathbb{C}\ |\ {\rm Re}\tau\leq t_{2}\};{\rm R}f^{w}_{!}(K|_{U}[N]))

(6.111)

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}(\{\tau\in\mathbb{C}\ |\ {\rm Re}\tau\leq t_{1}\};{\rm R}f^{w}_{!}(K|_{U}[N])).

(6.112)

This follows from
Kashiwara’s non-characteristic
deformation lemma (see
[
27
, Proposition 2.7.2]
)
as follows. Let
\iota:\mathbb{C}=\mathbb{R}\times\sqrt{-1}\mathbb{R}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{R}\times\sqrt{-1}\ \overline{\mathbb{R}}
be the
inclusion map. Then there exists
a continuous map

\ell:\mathbb{R}\times\sqrt{-1}\ \overline{\mathbb{R}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}

which extends the one
{\rm Re}:\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
.
Now, by applying Kashiwara’s non-characteristic
deformation lemma to the Morse function

\ell:\mathbb{R}\times\sqrt{-1}\ \overline{\mathbb{R}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
we obtain an isomorphism

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}(\{\tau\in\mathbb{R}\times\sqrt{-1}\ \overline{\mathbb{R}}\ |\ \ell(\tau)\leq t_{2}\};\iota_{!}{\rm R}f^{w}_{!}(K|_{U}[N]))

(6.113)

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}(\{\tau\in\mathbb{R}\times\sqrt{-1}\ \overline{\mathbb{R}}\ |\ \ell(\tau)\leq t_{1}\};\iota_{!}{\rm R}f^{w}_{!}(K|_{U}[N])),

(6.114)

which is equivalent to the desired one.
For
1\leq j\leq l
we define a closed
half space
G_{j}\subset\mathbb{C}
of
\mathbb{C}
by

G_{j}:=\{\tau\in\mathbb{C}\ |\ {\rm Re}\tau\geq\gamma_{j}\}\subset\mathbb{C}.

(6.115)

Then for any
1\leq j\leq l
we have
isomorphisms

\displaystyle H^{n}{\rm R}\Gamma_{G_{j}}({\rm R}f^{w}_{!}(K|_{U}[N]))_{c_{j}}

\displaystyle\simeq H^{n}\phi_{\tau-c_{j}}({\rm R}f^{w}_{!}(K|_{U}[N]))[-1]

(6.116)

\displaystyle\simeq\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}^{d_{j}}&(n=0)\\
\\
0&(\mbox{otherwise}).\end{cases}

(6.117)

Starting from the situation (
6.100
),
by Morse theory, we can show that
for any
t=\gamma_{j}={\rm Re}c_{j}\in\mathbb{R}

(
1\leq j\leq l
)
there exists
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
such that

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}(\{\tau\in\mathbb{C}\ |\ {\rm Re}\tau\leq t+\varepsilon\};{\rm R}f^{w}_{!}(K|_{U}[N]))

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}(\{\tau\in\mathbb{C}\ |\ {\rm Re}\tau\leq t\};{\rm R}f^{w}_{!}(K|_{U}[N]))

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}(\{\tau\in\mathbb{C}\ |\ {\rm Re}\tau\leq t-\varepsilon\};{\rm R}f^{w}_{!}(K|_{U}[N]))\oplus\mathbb{C}^{d_{j}}.

This implies that the restriction of
L(Sol_{X}(\mathcal{M}))

to the fiber
\pi^{-1}(w)\simeq\mathbb{R}
of
the point
w\in V\subset\Omega

is isomorphic to that of the sheaf

\displaystyle\Bigl(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\bigl(\mathbb{C}_{\{t+{\rm Re}g_{i}(w)\geq 0\}}\bigr)^{\oplus m(i)}\Bigr)\bigoplus\Bigl\{\bigoplus_{i=r+1}^{d}\Bigl(\bigoplus_{k=1}^{n_{i}}\bigl(\mathbb{C}_{\{t+{\rm Re}g_{ik}(w)\geq 0\}}\bigr)^{\oplus m(i,k)}\Bigr)\Bigr\}.

\displaystyle\simeq\Bigl(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\bigl({\rm E}_{V^{{\rm an}}|\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}g_{i}}\bigr)^{\oplus m(i)}\Bigr)\bigoplus\Bigl\{\bigoplus_{i=r+1}^{d}\Bigl(\bigoplus_{k=1}^{n_{i}}\bigl({\rm E}_{V^{{\rm an}}|\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}g_{ik}}\bigr)^{\oplus m(i,k)}\Bigr)\Bigr\}.

Since the subsets
(V\times\mathbb{R})\cap\{t+{\rm Re}g_{i}(w)\geq 0\}\simeq V\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and

(V\times\mathbb{R})\cap\{t+{\rm Re}g_{ik}(w)\geq 0\}\simeq V\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}

of
V\times\mathbb{R}

are connected and simply connected,
we can extend this isomorphism to the whole
V\times\mathbb{R}\subset Y^{{\rm an}}\times\mathbb{R}
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Corollary 6.20
.

In the situation of Theorem
6.19
,
the restriction of the Fourier transform

\mathcal{M}^{\wedge}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\rm hol}(\mathcal{D}_{Y})
of
\mathcal{M}

to
\Omega\subset Y=\mathbb{C}^{N}_{w}
is an integrable connection.
Moreover its rank is equal to

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{d}m(i)=\Bigl(\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{r}m(i)\Bigr)+\displaystyle\sum_{i=r+1}^{d}\Bigl(\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n_{i}}m(i,k)\Bigr).

(6.118)

Since the proof of this corollary is completely the
same as that of
[
22
, Corollary 4.5]
,
we omit it here. Next fix a point

w\in Y=\mathbb{C}^{N}
such that
w\neq 0
and set

{\mathbb{L}}:=\mathbb{C}w=\{\lambda w\ |\ \lambda\in\mathbb{C}\}\subset Y=\mathbb{C}^{N}.

Then
{\mathbb{L}}
is a complex line isomorphic to
\mathbb{C}_{\lambda}
.
Assume that
{\mathbb{L}}
is not contained in

D:=Y\setminus\Omega\subset Y=\mathbb{C}^{N}
.
Let
{\mathbb{P}}:={\mathbb{L}}\sqcup\{\infty\}\subset\overline{Y}={\mathbb{P}}^{N}

be the projective compactification of
{\mathbb{L}}

and
i_{{\mathbb{P}}}:{\mathbb{P}}\hookrightarrow\overline{Y}={\mathbb{P}}^{N}

the inclusion map. Then by Corollary
6.20

the holonomic D-module

\mathcal{L}:=H^{0}\mathbf{D}(i_{{\mathbb{P}}})^{\ast}\mathcal{M}^{\wedge}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize hol}}(\mathcal{D}_{\mathbb{P}})
on
{\mathbb{P}}
is a
meromorphic connection on a
neighborhood of the point
\infty\in{\mathbb{P}}
.
The following result is a generalization
of
[
14
, Theorem 5.6]

and
[
22
, Theorem 4.6]
.
Let
\varpi_{\mathbb{P}}:\widetilde{{\mathbb{P}}}\to{\mathbb{P}}

be the real oriented blow-up of
{\mathbb{P}}
along the divisor

\{\infty\}\subset{\mathbb{P}}
.

Theorem 6.21
.

In the situation of Theorem
6.19
,
for any point
\theta\in\varpi_{{\mathbb{P}}}^{-1}(\{\infty\})\simeq S^{1}

there exists its open neighborhood
W
in
\widetilde{{\mathbb{P}}}

such that we have an isomorphism

\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{A}}|_{W}\simeq\Bigr(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{d}\bigl((\mathcal{E}_{{\mathbb{L}}|{\mathbb{P}}}^{g_{i}(\lambda w)})^{\mathcal{A}}\bigr)^{\oplus m(i)}\Bigl)|_{W}

of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathcal{A}}
-modules
(see Section
7
for the definition)
on
W
. In particular,
the functions
g_{i}(\lambda w)

of
\lambda
are the exponential factors of
the meromorphic connection
\mathcal{L}
at the point

\infty\in{\mathbb{P}}
. Moreover the multiplicity of

g_{i}(\lambda w)
is equal
to
m(i)
.

Proof.

By
[
22
, Proposition 3.5]
,
for any point
\theta\in\varpi_{{\mathbb{P}}}^{-1}(\{\infty\})\simeq S^{1}

there exists its sectorial neighborhood

V_{\theta}\subset{\mathbb{P}}\backslash\{\infty\}
such that
we have isomorphisms

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V_{\theta}}\otimes Sol_{\mathbb{P}}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathbb{L}}|{\mathbb{P}}}^{g_{i}(\lambda w)})\simeq\mathbb{E}_{V_{\theta}|{\mathbb{P}}}^{{\rm Re}g_{i}(\lambda w)}\simeq\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V_{\theta}}\otimes\Big(\underset{a\to+\infty}{``\varinjlim"}\ \mathbb{C}_{\{t\geq-{\rm Re}g_{i}(\lambda w)+a\}}\Big).

(6.119)

On the other hand, by Theorem
6.19

there exists an isomorphism

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V_{\theta}}\otimes Sol_{\mathbb{P}}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{L})\simeq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{d}\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V_{\theta}}\otimes\Big(\underset{a\to+\infty}{``\varinjlim"}\ \mathbb{C}_{\{t\geq-{\rm Re}g_{i}(\lambda w)+a\}}^{\oplus m(i)}\Big).

(6.120)

We thus obtain an isomorphism

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V_{\theta}}\otimes Sol_{\mathbb{P}}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{L})\simeq\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V_{\theta}}\otimes Sol_{\mathbb{P}}^{\rm E}\Bigl(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{d}\big(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathbb{L}}|{\mathbb{P}}}^{g_{i}(\lambda w)}\big)^{\oplus m(i)}\Bigr).

Then the assertions follow from

[
22
, Corollary 3.11 and Theorem 3.18]
.
∎

As in Esterov-Takeuchi
[
14
, Remark 5.7]
,
by Theorems
6.19
and
6.21

we easily obtain the Stokes lines of the meromorphic connection

\mathcal{L}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize hol}}(\mathcal{D}_{\mathbb{P}})
at
\infty\in{\mathbb{P}}
.
We leave the precise formulation to the readers.

By Theorem
6.19
, at generic
points
v\in D=Y\setminus\Omega\subset Y=\mathbb{C}^{N}
at which

D
is a smooth hypersurface in
Y=\mathbb{C}^{N}

we obtain also the irregularity and the exponential
factors of
\mathcal{M}^{\wedge}
along it as follows.
Let
D_{\rm reg}\subset D
be
the smooth part of
D
and
v\in D_{\rm reg}

such a generic point.
Take a subvariety
M\subset Y
of
Y=\mathbb{C}^{N}
which
intersects
D_{\rm reg}
at
v
transversally.
We call it a normal slice of
D
at
v
.
By definition
M
is smooth and of dimension
1

on a neighborhood of
v
.
Let
i_{M}:M\xhookrightarrow{\ \ \ }Y=\mathbb{C}^{N}
be
the inclusion map and set

\mathcal{K}={\mathbf{D}}i_{M}^{\ast}\mathcal{M}^{\wedge}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize hol}}(\mathcal{D}_{M})
.
Then we can describe the irregularity

{\rm irr}(\mathcal{K}(\ast\{v\}))
of the
meromorphic connection

\mathcal{K}(\ast\{v\})
on
M
along
\{v\}\subset M

as follows. Recall that the irregularity

{\rm irr}(\mathcal{K}(\ast\{v\}))
is a non-negative integer and
equal to
-\chi_{v}\big(Sol_{M}(\mathcal{K}(\ast\{v\}))\big)
,
where

\chi_{v}\big(Sol_{M}(\mathcal{K}(\ast\{v\}))\big):=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{j}{\rm dim}H^{j}Sol_{M}(\mathcal{K}(\ast\{v\}))_{v}

(6.121)

is the local Euler-Poincaré index of

Sol_{M}(\mathcal{K}(\ast\{v\}))
at the point
v\in M

(see e.g. Sabbah
[
61
]
).
Shrinking the normal slice
M

if necessary we may assume that

M=\{u\in\mathbb{C}\ |\ |u|<\varepsilon\}
for some
\varepsilon>0
,

\{v\}=\{u=0\}
and

M\setminus\{v\}\subset\Omega
.
Let
i_{0}:M\setminus\{v\}\hookrightarrow\Omega

be the inclusion map and define
(possibly multi-valued) holomorphic
functions
\varphi_{i}:M\setminus\{v\}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
(
1\leq i\leq d
) by

\varphi_{i}(u)=g_{i}(i_{0}(u)).

(6.122)

Then it is easy to see that
\varphi_{i}(u)

are Laurent Puiseux series of
u

(see Kirwan
[
37
, Section 7.2]
etc.).
For each Laurent Puiseux series

\varphi_{i}(u)=\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Q}}c_{i,a}u^{a}\qquad(c_{i,a}\in\mathbb{C})

(6.123)

set
r_{i}=\min\{a\in\mathbb{Q}\ |\ c_{i,a}\not=0\}
and
define its pole order

{\rm ord}_{\{v\}}(\varphi_{i})\geq 0
by

{\rm ord}_{\{v\}}(\varphi_{i})=\begin{cases}-r_{i}&(r_{i}<0)\\
\\
0&(\mbox{\rm otherwise}).\end{cases}

(6.124)

Then we obtain the following theorem.

Theorem 6.22
.

The exponential factors appearing in the
Hukuhara-Levelt-Turrittin decomposition
of the meromorphic connection
\mathcal{K}(\ast\{v\})
at
v\in M

are the pole parts of
\varphi_{i}\ (1\leq i\leq d)
.
Moreover for any
1\leq i\leq d
the
multiplicity of the pole part of
\varphi_{i}

is equal to
m(i)
.
In particular, the irregularity of
the meromorphic connection
\mathcal{K}(\ast\{v\})
along
v\in M

is given by

{\rm irr}(\mathcal{K}(\ast\{v\}))=\sum_{i=1}^{d}m(i)\cdot{\rm ord}_{\{v\}}(\varphi_{i}).

(6.125)

7
Toward the Study of Fourier Transforms
of General Holonomic D-modules

7.1
Preliminary Results for Holonomic D-modules

In this subsection, we prove some new formulas
which might be useful to extend our results in
Section
6
to
arbitrary holonomic D-modules.
First of all, let us recall some notions and results in
[
9
, §7]
.
Let
X
be a complex manifold and
D\subset X
a
normal crossing divisor in it. Denote by

\varpi_{X}:\widetilde{X}\to X
the real oriented blow-up of
X
along

D
(sometimes we denote it simply by
\varpi
).
Then we set

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}}^{\rm t}

\displaystyle:={\rm R}{\mathcal{H}}om_{\varpi^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{\overline{X}}}(\varpi^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}},\mathcal{D}b_{\widetilde{X_{\mathbb{R}}}}^{\rm t}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{X}}

\displaystyle:=\alpha_{\widetilde{X}}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}}^{\rm t},

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\widetilde{X}}^{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{X}}\otimes_{\varpi^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varpi^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{X},

where
\mathcal{D}b_{\widetilde{X}}^{\rm t}
stands for the ind-sheaf
of tempered distributions on
\widetilde{X}

(for the definition see
[
9
, Notation 7.2.4]
).
Recall that a section of
\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{X}}
is a holomorphic function having
moderate growth at
\varpi_{X}^{-1}(D)
.
Note that
\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{X}}
and

\mathcal{D}_{\widetilde{X}}^{\mathcal{A}}
are sheaves of rings on
\widetilde{X}
.
For
\mathcal{M}\in{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{D}_{X})
we define
an object
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{A}}\in{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{D}_{\widetilde{X}}^{\mathcal{A}})
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{D}_{\widetilde{X}}^{\mathcal{A}}\overset{L}{\otimes}_{\varpi^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{X}}\varpi^{-1}\mathcal{M}\simeq\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{X}}\overset{L}{\otimes}_{\varpi^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varpi^{-1}\mathcal{M}.

Note that if
\mathcal{M}
is a holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module such that

\mathcal{M}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{M}(\ast D)
and

{\rm sing.supp}(\mathcal{M})\subset D
,
then one has
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{A}}\simeq\mathcal{D}_{\widetilde{X}}^{\mathcal{A}}\otimes_{\varpi^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{X}}\varpi^{-1}\mathcal{M}

(see
[
9
, Lemma 7.3.2]
).
Moreover we have an isomorphism
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{A}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{M}(\ast D)^{\mathcal{A}}

for any holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{M}
(see
[
9
, Lemma 7.2.2]
).
Let us take local coordinates

(u,v)=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{l},v_{1},\ldots,v_{n-l})

of
X
such that
D=\{u_{1}u_{2}\cdots u_{l}=0\}
.
We define a partial order
\leq
on the
set
\mathbb{Z}^{l}
by

a=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{l})\leq a^{\prime}=(a^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,a^{\prime}_{l})\ \Longleftrightarrow\ a_{i}\leq a_{i}^{\prime}\ (1\leq i\leq l).

Then for a meromorphic function
\varphi\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast D)

on
X
having a pole along
D
by using its Laurent expansion

\varphi=\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{l}}c_{a}(\varphi)(v)\cdot u^{a}\ \in\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast D)

with respect to
u_{1},\ldots,u_{l}

we define its order

{\rm ord}(\varphi)\in\mathbb{Z}^{l}
to be
the minimum

\min\Big(\{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{l}\ |\ c_{a}(\varphi)\not=0\}\cup\{0\}\Big)

if it exists. In
[
48
, Chapter 5]

Mochizuki defined the notion of
good sets of irregular values on
(X,D)
to be
finite subsets
S\subset\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast D)/\mathcal{O}_{X}
such that

(i):
{\rm ord}(\varphi)
exists for any

\varphi\in S
and if
\varphi\not=0
then its
leading term
c_{{\rm ord}(\varphi)}(\varphi)(v)

does not vanish
at any point
v\in Y:=\{u_{1}=\cdots=u_{l}=0\}\subset D
.

(ii):
{\rm ord}(\varphi-\psi)
exists for any

\varphi\not=\psi
in
S
and then
{\rm ord}(\varphi-\psi)\in\mathbb{Z}^{l}_{\leq 0}\setminus\{0\}
and the
leading term
c_{{\rm ord}(\varphi-\psi)}(\varphi-\psi)(v)

does not vanish
at any point
v\in Y=\{u_{1}=\cdots=u_{l}=0\}\subset D
.

(iii): the subset
\{{\rm ord}(\varphi-\psi)\ |\ \varphi,\psi\in S,\varphi\not=\psi\}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{l}

is totally ordered with respect to the order
\leq
on
\mathbb{Z}^{l}
.

Definition 7.1
.

Let
X
be a complex manifold and
D\subset X
a
normal crossing divisor in it.
Then we say that a holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module

\mathcal{M}
has a normal form along
D
if

(i)
\mathcal{M}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{M}(\ast D)

(ii)
\rm{sing.supp}(\mathcal{M})\subset D

(iii) for any
\theta\in\varpi^{-1}(D)\subset\widetilde{X}
,
there exist an open neighborhood
U\subset X

of
\varpi({\theta})\in D
in
X
, a good set

S=\{[\varphi_{1}],[\varphi_{2}],\ldots,[\varphi_{k}]\}\subset\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast D)/\mathcal{O}_{X}

(
\varphi_{i}\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast D)
)
of irregular values on
(U,D\cap U)
,
positive integers

m_{i}>0
(
1\leq i\leq k
) and
an open neighborhood
W
of
\theta

with
W\subset\varpi^{-1}(U)

such that

\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{A}}|_{W}\simeq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\Bigl(\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{U\backslash D|U}^{\varphi_{i}}\bigr)^{\mathcal{A}}|_{W}\Bigr)^{\oplus m_{i}}.

(7.1)

By
[
22
, Proposition 3.19]
the good set

S\subset\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast D)/\mathcal{O}_{X}
of
irregular values for
\mathcal{M}

in this definition does not depend on
the point
\theta\in\varpi^{-1}(D)
. Moreover
by
[
22
, Proposition 3.5]
for any

\theta\in\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)
there exists its
sectorial open neighborhood
V\subset U\setminus D

such that

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\Bigl(\mathbb{E}_{V|X}^{\rm Re\varphi_{i}}\Bigr)^{\oplus m_{i}}.

(7.2)

Lemma 7.2
.

In the situation as above,
there exists a
sectorial open neighborhood
V\subset U\setminus D

of
\theta\in\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)

such that for any
1\leq i,j\leq k
the natural morphism

{\rm Hom}^{\rm E}({\rm E}_{V|M}^{\rm Re\varphi_{i}},{\rm E}_{V|M}^{\rm Re\varphi_{j}})\longrightarrow{\rm Hom}^{\rm E}(\mathbb{E}_{V|X}^{\rm Re\varphi_{i}},\mathbb{E}_{V|X}^{\rm Re\varphi_{j}})

(7.3)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of
[
10
, Lemma 5.2.1 (ii)]
.
It suffices to consider only pairs
(i,j)
such that

i\not=j
. Then by
[\varphi_{i}]\not=[\varphi_{j}]

the function
\varphi_{j}-\varphi_{i}\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast D)
has a pole along the normal
crossing divisor
D\subset X
.
For a local coordinate system

(u,v)=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{l},v_{1},\ldots,v_{n-l})

of
X
such that
D=\{u_{1}u_{2}\cdots u_{l}=0\}

and
\varpi(\theta)\in Y:=\{u_{1}=u_{2}=\cdots=u_{l}=0\}\subset D
let

(\varphi_{j}-\varphi_{i})(u,v)=\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{l}}c_{a}(\varphi_{j}-\varphi_{i})(v)\cdot u^{a}\ \in\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast D)

(7.4)

be the Laurent expansion of
\varphi_{j}-\varphi_{i}

with respect to
u_{1},u_{2},\ldots,u_{l}
.
Then by the goodness of the set
S
the order

\alpha:={\rm ord}(\varphi_{j}-\varphi_{i})\in\mathbb{Z}^{l}

is defined and satisfies the condition

\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\ldots,\alpha_{l})\quad\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0})^{l}\setminus\{0\}.

(7.5)

If there exists a sectorial open neighborhood

V\subset X\setminus D

of
\theta\in\varpi^{-1}(D)

such that
\rm Re(\varphi_{j}-\varphi_{i})\leq 0
on
V
,
then by the proof of
[
10
, Lemmas 3.1.1 and 3.2.2]

we have isomorphisms

{\rm Hom}^{\rm E}({\rm E}_{V|M}^{\rm Re\varphi_{i}},{\rm E}_{V|M}^{\rm Re\varphi_{j}})\simeq\mathbb{C},\quad{\rm Hom}^{\rm E}(\mathbb{E}_{V|X}^{\rm Re\varphi_{i}},\mathbb{E}_{V|X}^{\rm Re\varphi_{j}})\simeq\mathbb{C}.

(7.6)

Otherwise, the point
\theta\in\varpi^{-1}(D)
is
contained in the closure
\overline{R}
of the open subset

R:=\{(u,v)\in X\setminus D\ |\ \rm Re(\varphi_{j}-\varphi_{i})(u,v)>0\}

(7.7)

of
\widetilde{X}
. As the meromorphic function

\varphi_{j}-\varphi_{i}\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast D)
has no point of indeterminacy by
the goodness of the set
S
, we can easily
show that
R
is a subanalytic open
subset of
\widetilde{X}
. Then by the curve selection lemma
there exists a
real analytic curve
\gamma(t):[0,\varepsilon)\longrightarrow\overline{R}
(
\varepsilon>0
)
(defined on a neighborhood of
0\in\mathbb{R}
)
such that
\gamma(0)=\theta\in\varpi^{-1}(D)\cap\overline{R}
and

\gamma(t)\in R
for any
t\in(0,\varepsilon)
.
By the leading term

c_{\alpha}(\varphi_{j}-\varphi_{i})(v)\cdot u^{\alpha}

of the Laurent expansion of
\varphi_{j}-\varphi_{i}
,
we define a complex valued real analytic
function
\psi:\varpi^{-1}(Y)\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}

on the real analytic manifold

\varpi^{-1}(Y)\simeq(S^{1})^{l}\times Y

(defined on a neighborhood of the point

\theta\in\varpi^{-1}(Y)
) by

\psi(e^{i\delta_{1}},e^{i\delta_{2}},\ldots,e^{i\delta_{l}},v):=c_{\alpha}(\varphi_{j}-\varphi_{i})(v)\cdot e^{i\alpha_{1}\delta_{1}}e^{i\alpha_{2}\delta_{2}}\cdots e^{i\alpha_{l}\delta_{l}}.

(7.8)

Recall that the holomorphic function

c_{\alpha}(\varphi_{j}-\varphi_{i})
on
Y

does not vanish at any point
v\in Y
.
Suppose that
\rm Re\psi(\theta)<0
. Then
for the real analytic curve
\gamma
we have

\lim_{t\to+0}\rm Re(\varphi_{j}-\varphi_{i})(\gamma(t))=-\infty.

(7.9)

Then contradicts to the condition

\gamma(t)\in R
(
t\in(0,\varepsilon)
).
We thus obtain
\rm Re\psi(\theta)\geq 0
.
Then, by the above definition of
\psi
,
for any sectorial open neighborhood
V\subset X\setminus D

of
\theta\in\varpi^{-1}(Y)
there exists
a point
\theta^{\prime}=(e^{i\delta_{1}},\ldots,e^{i\delta_{l}},v)\in\varpi^{-1}(Y)\cap{\rm Int}(\overline{V})

such that
\rm Re\psi(\theta^{\prime})>0
.
Define a line

\gamma^{\prime}(t):(0,\varepsilon^{\prime})\longrightarrow V
(
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
) in
V
by

\gamma^{\prime}(t)=(te^{i\delta_{1}},te^{i\delta_{2}},\ldots,te^{i\delta_{l}},v)\in V\qquad(0<t<\varepsilon^{\prime})

(7.10)

so that we have

\lim_{t\to+0}\gamma^{\prime}(t)=\theta^{\prime}=(e^{i\delta_{1}},\ldots,e^{i\delta_{l}},v).

(7.11)

Then by
\rm Re\psi(\theta^{\prime})>0
we obtain

\lim_{t\to+0}\rm Re(\varphi_{j}-\varphi_{i})(\gamma^{\prime}(t))=+\infty,

(7.12)

which implies also that
\theta^{\prime}\in\overline{R}
.
By the proof of
[
10
, Lemma 3.1.1 (i)]

there exist isomorphisms

{\rm Hom}^{\rm E}({\rm E}_{V|M}^{\rm Re\varphi_{i}},{\rm E}_{V|M}^{\rm Re\varphi_{j}})\simeq{\rm Hom}_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(\mathbb{C}_{V\cap\{\rm Re(\varphi_{j}-\varphi_{i})\leq 0\}},\mathbb{C}_{X})\simeq 0.

(7.13)

Moreover by the proof of
[
10
, Lemma 3.2.2 (i)]

we have

{\rm Hom}^{\rm E}(\mathbb{E}_{V|X}^{\rm Re\varphi_{i}},\mathbb{E}_{V|X}^{\rm Re\varphi_{j}})\simeq\varinjlim_{c\to+\infty}{\rm Hom}_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(\mathbb{C}_{V\cap\{\rm Re(\varphi_{j}-\varphi_{i})\leq c\}},\mathbb{C}_{X})\simeq 0.

(7.14)

We thus obtain the desired isomorphism

{\rm Hom}^{\rm E}({\rm E}_{V|M}^{\rm Re\varphi_{i}},{\rm E}_{V|M}^{\rm Re\varphi_{j}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm Hom}^{\rm E}(\mathbb{E}_{V|X}^{\rm Re\varphi_{i}},\mathbb{E}_{V|X}^{\rm Re\varphi_{j}})

(7.15)

for the pair
(i.j)
. Clearly we can take a
sectorial open neighborhood
V\subset X\setminus D

of
\theta\in\varpi^{-1}(D)

so that this isomorphism holds for any
pair
(i.j)
.
∎

A ramification of
X
along the normal crossing divisor

D\subset X
on a neighborhood
U

of
x\in D
is a finite map
\rho:X^{\prime}\longrightarrow U

of complex manifolds
of the form
w\longmapsto z=(z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{n})=\rho(w)=(w^{d_{1}}_{1},\ldots,w^{d_{l}}_{l},w_{l+1},\ldots,w_{n})

for some
(d_{1},\ldots,d_{l})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{>0})^{l}
, where

(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})
is a local coordinate system of
X^{\prime}
and

(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})
is that of

U
such that
D\cap U=\{z_{1}\cdots z_{l}=0\}
.

Definition 7.3
.

Let
X
be a complex manifold and
D\subset X
a
normal crossing divisor in it.
Then we say that a holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{M}
has a
quasi-normal form along
D
if it satisfies
the conditions (i) and (ii) of Definition
7.1
,
and if for any point
x\in D

there exists a ramification
\rho:X^{\prime}\to U

on a neighborhood
U
of it such that
\mathbf{D}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{M}|_{U})

has a normal form along the normal crossing divisor

\rho^{-1}(D\cap U)
.

Note that
\mathbf{D}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{M}|_{U})
as well
as
\mathbf{D}\rho_{\ast}\mathbf{D}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{M}|_{U})

is concentrated in degree zero and
\mathcal{M}|_{U}
is a
direct summand of
\mathbf{D}\rho_{\ast}\mathbf{D}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{M}|_{U})
.
Now let
\mathcal{M}
be a holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module having a
quasi-normal form along the normal crossing divisor

D\subset X
. Then for any point
x\in D

there exists a ramification
\rho:X^{\prime}\to U

on a neighborhood
U
of it such that
\mathbf{D}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{M}|_{U})

has a normal form along the normal crossing divisor

D^{\prime}:=\rho^{-1}(D\cap U)\subset X^{\prime}
.
Note that
\rho^{-1}(x)\subset D^{\prime}
is a point and
denote it by
x^{\prime}
.
Let
\varpi^{\prime}:\widetilde{X^{\prime}}\to X^{\prime}
be the real
oriented blow-up of
X^{\prime}
along

D^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{\rho}:\widetilde{X^{\prime}}\to\widetilde{X}
the morphism induced by
\rho
.
Then by
[
22
, Propositions 3.5 and 3.19]

there exist a unique good set

S=\{[\varphi_{1}],[\varphi_{2}],\ldots,[\varphi_{k}]\}\subset\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}(\ast D^{\prime})/\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}

(
\varphi_{i}\in\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}(\ast D^{\prime})
)
of irregular values on a neighborhood of

x^{\prime}\in D^{\prime}
in
X^{\prime}
and
positive integers

m_{i}>0
(
1\leq i\leq k
) such that
for any
\theta^{\prime}\in(\varpi^{\prime})^{-1}(D^{\prime})
and
its sufficiently small sectorial open neighborhood

V^{\prime}\subset X^{\prime}\setminus D^{\prime}

we have an isomorphism

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V^{\prime}}\otimes Sol_{X^{\prime}}^{\rm E}(\mathbf{D}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{M}|_{U}))\simeq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\Bigl(\mathbb{E}_{V^{\prime}|X^{\prime}}^{\rm Re\varphi_{i}}\Bigr)^{\oplus m_{i}}.

(7.16)

For a point
\theta\in\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)
and
its sufficiently small sectorial open neighborhood

V\subset U\setminus D
we take a point

\theta^{\prime}\in(\varpi^{\prime})^{-1}(D^{\prime})
such that
\widetilde{\rho}(\theta^{\prime})=\theta
and its sectorial open neighborhood

V^{\prime}\subset X^{\prime}\setminus D^{\prime}

such that
\rho|_{V^{\prime}}:V^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}V
.
Define holomorphic functions

f_{i}:V\to\mathbb{C}
(
1\leq i\leq k
) by

f_{i}:=\varphi_{i}\circ(\rho|_{V^{\prime}})^{-1}
.
Then by
[
22
, Proposition 3.5]
we obtain
an isomorphism

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\Bigl(\mathbb{E}_{V|X}^{\rm Ref_{i}}\Bigr)^{\oplus m_{i}}.

(7.17)

As
\widetilde{\rho}:\widetilde{X^{\prime}}\to\widetilde{X}
is locally an isomorphism, then it is
also clear that on an open neighborhood
W
of
\theta

in
\widetilde{X}
we have an isomorphism

\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{A}}|_{W}\simeq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\Bigl(\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{U\backslash D|U}^{f_{i}}\bigr)^{\mathcal{A}}|_{W}\Bigr)^{\oplus m_{i}}.

(7.18)

Moreover, by the proof of Lemma
7.2

we obtain the following result.

Lemma 7.4
.

In the situation as above,
there exists a
sectorial open neighborhood
V\subset U\setminus D

of
\theta\in\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)

such that for any
1\leq i,j\leq k
the natural morphism

{\rm Hom}^{\rm E}({\rm E}_{V|M}^{\rm Ref_{i}},{\rm E}_{V|M}^{\rm Ref_{j}})\longrightarrow{\rm Hom}^{\rm E}(\mathbb{E}_{V|X}^{\rm Ref_{i}},\mathbb{E}_{V|X}^{\rm Ref_{j}})

(7.19)

is an isomorphism.

In order to improve (
7.17
) and obtain a
higher-dimensional analogue of
D’Agnolo-Kashiwara
[
10
, Proposition 5.4.5]
,
let us prepare some notations (see
[
10
, Section 5]

for the details in the one dimensional case). For
the real oriented blow-up

\varpi:\widetilde{X}\to X
of
X
along the normal crossing divisor

D\subset X
consider the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\varpi^{-1}(D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\imath}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varpi}
\textstyle{X\backslash D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\jmath}}
\textstyle{X,}

(7.20)

where
\widetilde{\imath},\widetilde{\jmath},j

are the natural embeddings. For an open subset
\Omega\subset\widetilde{X}
,

f\in\Gamma(\Omega;\widetilde{\jmath}_{\ast}j^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X})\simeq\Gamma(\widetilde{\jmath}^{-1}\Omega;\mathcal{O}_{X\setminus D})
and
\theta\in\Omega\cap\varpi^{-1}(D)

we say that
f
admits a Puiseux expansion
along
D\subset X
at
\theta
if there exist
a ramification
\rho:X^{\prime}\to U

of a neighborhood
U
of
\varpi(\theta)\in D

along
D\cap U\subset U
, a sectorial
neighborhood
V\subset U\setminus D
of
\theta

contained in
\widetilde{\jmath}^{-1}\Omega=\varpi(\Omega\setminus\varpi^{-1}(D))\subset X\setminus D
and a
meromorphic function
g\in\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}(*D^{\prime})

along the normal crossing divisor
D^{\prime}:=\rho^{-1}(D)\subset X^{\prime}
defined on an open neighborhood

W
of
\rho^{-1}(\overline{V}\cap D)=\overline{\rho^{-1}(V)}\cap D^{\prime}
in
X^{\prime}
such that
the pull-back of
f|_{V}\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)
by
\rho
conicides
with
g
on the open subset
W\cap\rho^{-1}(V)\subset W
.
We denote by
\mathcal{P}_{\widetilde{X}}
the subsheaf of

\widetilde{\jmath}_{\ast}j^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}
whose sections are defined by

\Gamma(\Omega;\mathcal{P}_{\widetilde{X}})\coloneq\{f\in\Gamma(\Omega;\widetilde{\jmath}_{\ast}j^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X})\mid\textit{For any $\theta\in\Omega\cap\varpi^{-1}(D)$,}\\
\textit{$f$ admits a Puiseux expansion along $D\subset X$ at $\theta$.}\}

for open subsets
\Omega\subset\widetilde{X}
.
Then we define the sheaf of
Puiseux germs
\mathcal{P}_{\varpi^{-1}(D)}
on
\varpi^{-1}(D)
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\varpi^{-1}(D)}\coloneq\widetilde{\imath}^{\,-1}\mathcal{P}_{\widetilde{X}}.

(7.21)

For a point
\theta\in\varpi^{-1}(D)
if we take a local coordinate

(u,v)=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{l},v_{1},\ldots,v_{n-l})
of
X

on a neighborhood of
\varpi(\theta)\in D
in
X
such that

\varpi(\theta)=(0,0)\in D=\{u_{1}u_{2}\cdots u_{l}=0\}

then the stalk of
\mathcal{P}_{\varpi^{-1}(D)}
at
\theta

is isomorphic to the ring

\bigcup_{p\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}}\ \mathbb{C}\{u_{1}^{\frac{1}{p}},\ldots,u_{l}^{\frac{1}{p}},v_{1},\ldots,v_{n-l}\}\bigl[u_{1}^{-\frac{1}{p}},\ldots,u_{l}^{-\frac{1}{p}}\bigr].

(7.22)

of Puiseux series along
D\subset X
. We
denote by
\mathcal{P}_{\varpi^{-1}(D)}^{\leq 0}
the subsheaf of

\mathcal{P}_{\varpi^{-1}(D)}
consisting of sections locally contained in
the ring

\bigcup_{p\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}}\ \mathbb{C}\{u_{1}^{\frac{1}{p}},\ldots,u_{l}^{\frac{1}{p}},v_{1},\ldots,v_{n-l}\}

(7.23)

for some (hence, any) local coordinate

(u,v)=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{l},v_{1},\ldots,v_{n-l})
of
X

as above. By this definition, it is clear that
for any point
x\in D
there exist its neighborhood

U
in
X
and a subsheaf
\mathcal{P}_{\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{P}_{\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)}
of
\mathbb{C}_{\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)}
-modules
defined on
the open subset
\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)\subset\varpi^{-1}(D)

such that the natural morphism

\mathcal{P}_{\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)}/\mathcal{P}_{\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)}^{\leq 0}

(7.24)

is an isomorphism. We call such
\mathcal{P}_{\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)}^{\prime}

a representative subsheaf of
\mathcal{P}_{\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)}
.
By slightly modifying the definition of the multiplicities in
D’Agnolo-Kashiwara
[
10
, Section 5.3]
, we shall use
the following one (cf.
[
39
, Definition 2.4]
).

Definition 7.5
.

(cf.
[
10
, Section 5.3]
and
[
39
, Definition 2.4]
)
In the situation as above, we say that a morphism

N:\mathcal{P}_{\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)}^{\prime}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})_{\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)}
of sheaves of
sets is a multiplicity along
D\cap U\subset U
if
there exists
a ramification
\rho:X^{\prime}\to U

of
U
along
D\cap U\subset U
such that
for any
\theta\in\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)
the subset

N_{\theta}^{>0}:=N_{\theta}^{-1}(\mathbb{Z}_{>0})\subset\mathcal{P}_{\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U),\theta}^{\prime}
is finite
and the pull-backs of its elements

f\in N_{\theta}^{>0}
by
\rho
are meromorphic
functions on
X^{\prime}
along
D^{\prime}:=\rho^{-1}(D)\subset X^{\prime}
and form a good set

\{[f\circ\rho]\ |\ f\in N_{\theta}^{>0}\}\subset\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}(\ast D^{\prime})/\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}

of irregular values on
(X^{\prime},D^{\prime})

on a neighborhood of the point
\rho^{-1}(\varpi(\theta))\in D^{\prime}
.

Definition 7.6
.

(cf.
[
10
, Definition 5.3.1]
and
[
39
, Definition 2.5]
)
In the situation as above, we say that an

\mathbb{R}
-constructible enhanced sheaf

F\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{X})
on
X

has a quasi-normal form along the normal crossing
divisor
D\cap U\subset U
if there exists a
multiplicity
N:\mathcal{P}_{\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)}^{\prime}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})_{\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)}

such that any point
\theta\in\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)
has
its sectorial open neighborhood

V_{\theta}\subset U\setminus D\subset\widetilde{X}
for
which we have an isomorphism

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V_{\theta}}\otimes F\simeq\bigoplus_{f\in N_{\theta}^{>0}}\Bigl({\rm E}_{V_{\theta}|X}^{\rm Ref}\Bigr)^{N(f)}.

(7.25)

Enhanced ind-sheaves having a quasi-normal form along the
normal crossing divisor
D\cap U\subset U
are defined similarly.

Lemma 7.7
.

Assume that a holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{M}
has a
quasi-normal form along the normal crossing
divisor
D\subset X
. Then for any point
x\in D

there exist a subanalytic open neighborhood

U
of
x
in
X
such that the

\mathbb{R}
-constructible enhanced ind-sheaf

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{U}\otimes Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{U\setminus D}\otimes Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M})

(7.26)

has a quasi-normal form along the normal crossing
divisor
D\cap U\subset U
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of
[
10
, Lemma 5.4.4]
.
With the representative subsheaf
\mathcal{P}_{\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)}^{\prime}

of
\mathcal{P}_{\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)}
at hands, it suffices to use
(
7.17
), (
7.18
) and

[
22
, Propositions 3.10 and 3.19]
.
∎

In the situation of Lemma
7.7
, let

N:\mathcal{P}_{\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)}^{\prime}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})_{\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)}
be the multiplicity
for which the enhanced ind-sheaf

\mathcal{F}\simeq\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{U}\otimes Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M})\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}({\rm I}\mathbb{C}_{X})
has a quasi-normal form along
the normal crossing divisor
D\cap U\subset U
. Then
by the proof of Lemma
7.7
, the sections of
the subsheaf
N^{>0}=N^{-1}((\mathbb{Z}_{>0})_{\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)})\subset\mathcal{P}_{\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)}^{\prime}
are
the exponential factors of
\mathcal{M}
. Moreover, if
the divisor
D\cap U\subset U
is smooth and connected,
then the non-negative rational number

\displaystyle\sum_{f\in N_{\theta}^{>0}}N_{\theta}(f)\cdot{\rm ord}_{D\cap U}(f)\quad\in\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}

(7.27)

associated to a point
\theta\in\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)

is an integer and does not depend on the choice of

\theta\in\varpi^{-1}(D\cap U)
, where for
the exponential factor
f\in N_{\theta}^{>0}
of
\mathcal{M}

the rational number
{\rm ord}_{D\cap U}(f)\geq 0
stands for
the pole order of
f
along
D\cap U
. We call it the
irregularity of
\mathcal{M}
along
D\cap U
and
denote it by
{\rm irr}_{D\cap U}(\mathcal{M})
.
If
D\subset X
itself is smooth and connected,
we define the irregularity
{\rm irr}_{D}(\mathcal{M})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
of
\mathcal{M}
along
D\subset X
similarly.
By Lemmas
7.4
and
7.7
,
we obtain the following higher-dimensional analogue
of
[
10
, Proposition 5.4.5]
.
For a precise explanation of the proof of

[
10
, Proposition 5.4.5]
, see
[
39
, Remark 2.10]
.

Proposition 7.8
.

Assume that a holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{M}
has a
quasi-normal form along the normal crossing
divisor
D\subset X
. Then for any point
x\in D

there exist a subanalytic open neighborhood

U
of
x
in
X
and an

\mathbb{R}
-constructible enhanced sheaf

F\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{X})
on
X

having a quasi-normal form along the normal crossing
divisor
D\cap U\subset U

such that

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{U}\otimes Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{U\setminus D}\otimes Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\mathbb{C}_{X}^{\rm E}\overset{+}{\otimes}F.

(7.28)

The following fundamental result is due to
Kedlaya and Mochizuki.

Theorem 7.9

(
[
35
,
36
,
48
]
)
.

For a holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{M}
and
x\in X
,
there exist an open neighborhood
U
of
x
,
a closed hypersurface
Y\subset U
,
a complex manifold
X^{\prime}
and
a projective morphism
\nu:X^{\prime}\to U
such that

(i)

\rm{sing.supp}(\mathcal{M})\cap U\subset Y
,

(ii)

D:=\nu^{-1}(Y)
is a normal crossing divisor in
X^{\prime}
,

(iii)

\nu
induces an isomorphism
X^{\prime}\backslash D\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}U\backslash Y
,

(iv)

(\mathbf{D}\nu^{\ast}\mathcal{M})(\ast D)
has a quasi-normal form along
D
.

This is a generalization of the classical
Hukuhara-Levelt-Turrittin theorem to higher dimensions.
Now let
X
be a compact complex manifold,

Y\subset X
a closed hypersurface and

\mathcal{M}
a holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module such that

\rm{sing.supp}(\mathcal{M})\subset Y
and

\mathcal{M}(*Y)\simeq\mathcal{M}
. Assume that there exist a
projective morphism
\nu:Z\to X

of a compact complex manifold
Z
such that

D:=\nu^{-1}(Y)\subset Z
is a normal
crossing divisor and
\nu|_{Z\setminus D}:Z\setminus D\to X\setminus Y
is an
isomorphism. Assume also that there exist a
ramification
\rho:Z^{\prime}\to\mathcal{U}

on a neighborhood
\mathcal{U}
of
D
in
Z
,
meromorphic functions

\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\ldots,\varphi_{k}\in\Gamma\bigl(Z^{\prime}\ ;\ \mathcal{O}_{Z^{\prime}}(\ast\rho^{-1}(D))\bigr)

(7.29)

and positive integers
m_{i}>0
(
1\leq i\leq k
)
such that
\mathbf{D}\rho^{\ast}(\mathbf{D}\nu^{\ast}\mathcal{M}|_{\mathcal{U}})

has a normal form along the normal crossing divisor

D^{\prime}:=\rho^{-1}(D)\subset Z^{\prime}

for the set

S=\{[\varphi_{1}],[\varphi_{2}],\ldots,[\varphi_{k}]\}\subset\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\prime}}(\ast D^{\prime})/\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\prime}}

(7.30)

which is good at each point of
D^{\prime}

and
m_{i}>0
(
1\leq i\leq k
). Then by the
proof of Proposition
7.8

we can also show that there
exist a semi-analytic open neighborhood

\mathcal{V}\subset\mathcal{U}
of
D
in
Z
and an

\mathbb{R}
-constructible enhanced sheaf

F\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{Z})
on
Z

having a quasi-normal form along the normal crossing
divisor
D
at each point of
D

such that

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{V}\setminus D}\otimes Sol_{Z}^{\rm E}(\mathbf{D}\nu^{\ast}\mathcal{M})\simeq\mathbb{C}_{Z}^{\rm E}\overset{+}{\otimes}F.

(7.31)

Since in this situation there exists an isomorphism

\mathcal{M}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{D}\nu_{\ast}(\mathbf{D}\nu^{\ast}\mathcal{M}),

(7.32)

for the
\mathbb{R}
-constructible enhanced sheaf

G:=\mathbf{E}\nu_{\ast}F\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{X})
on
X

and the open neighborhood
W:=\nu(\mathcal{V})
of

Y
in
X
we obtain an isomorphism

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{W\setminus Y}\otimes Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\mathbb{C}_{X}^{\rm E}\overset{+}{\otimes}G.

(7.33)

As in Kudomi-Takeuchi
[
39
]
,
one can also slightly modify the

\mathbb{R}
-constructible enhanced sheaf

G\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{X})
so that we have isomorphisms

Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{X\setminus Y}\otimes Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\mathbb{C}_{X}^{\rm E}\overset{+}{\otimes}G.

(7.34)

By this very explicit description of
the enhanced solution complex

Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M})
, in the one dimensional
case
d_{X}={\rm dim}X=1
we can apply the
Morse theoretical argument in
[
22
]

to improve the main results in

[
10
]
. See
[
39
]
for the details.

7.2
Fourier Transforms of Standard Holonomic D-modules

In this subsection, let
X
be the affine space
\mathbb{C}^{N}
of
dimension
N
and regard it as an algebraic variety endowed
with the Zariski topology. Let
S\subset X
be a smooth
and connected quasi-affine subvariety of
X=\mathbb{C}^{N}

of dimension
n
and
\mathcal{N}
an algebraic integrable connection
on it. For some technical reason, we assume here that there exists
an algebraic hypersurface
H\subset X
of
X
such that

S\subset X\setminus H
and
S
is closed in
X\setminus H
.
This in particular implies that the inclusion map

i_{S}:S\hookrightarrow X
is affine. Then we set

\mathcal{M}:=\mathbf{D}i_{S*}\mathcal{N}\simeq i_{S*}\mathcal{N}\quad\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize hol}}(\mathcal{D}_{X}).

(7.35)

By the standard operations for algebraic D-modules,
for the initial study of Fourier transforms of general
holonomic D-modules on
X=\mathbb{C}^{N}
it suffices to
study those for such holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-modules. Recall that the Fourier
transform is an exact functor.
For this reason, let us call

\mathcal{M}
a standard holonomic D-module on
X
.
Let
\overline{X}={\mathbb{P}}^{N}
be the projective compactification
of
X=\mathbb{C}^{N}
and
\overline{S}\subset\overline{X}
the closure
of
S
in it. Let
\nu:Z\longrightarrow\overline{X}
be a
proper morphism of a smooth variety
Z
such that

\nu(Z)=\overline{S}
, the restriction
\nu^{-1}S\longrightarrow S
of
\nu
is an isomorphism and

D:=\nu^{-1}(\overline{S}\setminus S)=Z\setminus\nu^{-1}S\subset Z
is a normal crossing
divisor in
Z
. Let
i_{S}^{\prime}:S\simeq\nu^{-1}S\hookrightarrow Z
be the inclusion map and
consider the algebraic meromorphic connection

\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:=\mathbf{D}i_{S*}^{\prime}\mathcal{N}\simeq i_{S*}^{\prime}\mathcal{N}\quad\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize hol}}(\mathcal{D}_{Z})

(7.36)

on
Z
for which we have an isomorphism

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\simeq\mathbf{D}\nu_{\ast}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}.

(7.37)

Assume that the analytification
(\mathcal{M}^{\prime})^{{\rm an}}

of
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
on
Z^{{\rm an}}
has a quasi-normal form
along the normal crossing divisor
D^{{\rm an}}\subset Z^{{\rm an}}

and satisfies the nice property that we assumed at the
end of Subsection
7.1
. Then
by the proof of Proposition
7.8
there
exists a semi-analytic open neighborhood
\mathcal{V}
of
D^{{\rm an}}

in
Z^{{\rm an}}
and an
\mathbb{R}
-constructible enhanced
sheaf
F\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{Z^{{\rm an}}})
on
Z^{{\rm an}}

such that we have an isomorphism

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}}}\otimes Sol_{Z}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M}^{\prime})\simeq\mathbb{C}_{Z^{{\rm an}}}^{\rm E}\overset{+}{\otimes}F.

(7.38)

For the
\mathbb{R}
-constructible enhanced sheaf

G:=\mathbf{E}\nu_{\ast}F\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}})
on
\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}

and the semi-analytic open subset
\mathcal{W}:=\nu(\mathcal{V})\subset\overline{S}^{{\rm an}}
of

\overline{S}^{{\rm an}}
containing
\partial S:=(\overline{S}\setminus S)^{{\rm an}}

we thus obtain an isomorphism

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{W}\setminus\partial S}\otimes Sol_{\overline{X}}^{\rm E}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}})\simeq\mathbb{C}_{\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}}^{\rm E}\overset{+}{\otimes}G.

(7.39)

Let us explain the structure of
G
more precisely.
First, by the consruction of
F
there exist
(possibly multi-valued) holomorphic functions

f_{i}:\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}

(
1\leq i\leq k
)
on
\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}}
and positive integers

m_{i}>0
(
1\leq i\leq k
) for which the
enhanced sheaf
F\in{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{Z^{{\rm an}}})
has a
quasi-normal form along the normal crossing
divisor
D^{{\rm an}}\subset Z^{{\rm an}}
in the sense of
Definition
7.6
. Recall that we
constructed
F
by glueing some enhanced
sheaves
F_{j}
on open sectors
V_{j}\subset\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}}\subset Z^{{\rm an}}\setminus D^{{\rm an}}
along
D^{{\rm an}}

and for any
1\leq i\leq k
the analytic
continuation of
f_{i}
along any curve
\gamma\subset\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}}
concides with

f_{j}
for some
1\leq j\leq k
(see the
proof of
[
10
, Proposition 5.4.5]
).
Then we define a complex hypersurface

\widetilde{\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}}}\subset(\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}})\times\mathbb{C}
of

(\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}})\times\mathbb{C}
by

\widetilde{\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}}}:=\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}\ \{(x,-f_{i}(x))\ |\ x\in\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}}\}\quad\subset(\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}})\times\mathbb{C},

(7.40)

where in the right hand side we take the union
considering all the possible branches of
the (possibly multi-valued) functions
f_{i}
.
Since the pull-backs of
f_{i}
(
1\leq i\leq k
)
by a ramification
\rho:Z^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}
of
\mathcal{V}
along the normal crossing divisor

D^{{\rm an}}\subset\mathcal{V}
are single-valued and form
a good set of irregular values in the sense of
Mochizuki
[
48
, Chapter 5]
,
after shrinking
\mathcal{V}
if necessary we may assume that

\widetilde{\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}}}
is a smooth hypersurface in

(\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}})\times\mathbb{C}
.
Then by using the positive integers

m_{i}>0
(
1\leq i\leq k
) and the transition
matrices that we used to glue the enhanced sheave
F_{j}
in
the construction of
F
we define a local system

L
on the smooth hypersurface

\widetilde{\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}}}\subset(\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}})\times\mathbb{C}
. Let

i:\widetilde{\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{V}\times\mathbb{C}
be the (not necessarily closed)
embedding of
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}}}
into

\mathcal{V}\times\mathbb{C}
and set

\mathcal{F}:=i_{!}L\quad\in{\bf D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{V}\times\mathbb{C}).

(7.41)

Lemma 7.10
.

The object
\mathcal{F}[n]\in{\bf D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{V}\times\mathbb{C})
is
a perverse sheaf on
\mathcal{V}\times\mathbb{C}
.

Proof.

Let
\rho:Z^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}
be a ramification
of
\mathcal{V}
along the normal crossing divisor

D^{{\rm an}}\subset\mathcal{V}
such that the function

f_{i}\circ\rho
is single-valued for any

1\leq i\leq k
and
\rho\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{C}}:Z^{\prime}\times\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}\times\mathbb{C}
the morphism associated to it.
Then by the proof of Theorem
2.4
(i),

(\rho\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{C}})^{-1}\mathcal{F}

is a constructible sheaf on
Z^{\prime}\times\mathbb{C}
.
Moreover we can easily see that the canonical morphism

\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow(\rho\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{C}})_{*}(\rho\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{C}})^{-1}\mathcal{F}

(7.42)

of sheaves is injective and hence
\mathcal{F}
is a
subsheaf of the constructible sheaf

(\rho\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{C}})_{*}(\rho\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{C}})^{-1}\mathcal{F}
.
As the support of
\mathcal{F}
conicides with that of

(\rho\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{C}})_{*}(\rho\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{C}})^{-1}\mathcal{F}
in

(\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}})\times\mathbb{C}\subset\mathcal{V}\times\mathbb{C}
and
\mathcal{F}|_{D^{{\rm an}}\times\mathbb{C}}\simeq 0
, we then see that
\mathcal{F}
itself is
constructible. It follows that the restriction of

\mathcal{F}[n]
to
(\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}})\times\mathbb{C}

is perverse. Now let
j:(\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}})\times\mathbb{C}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{V}\times\mathbb{C}
be the inclusion map.
Then there exists an isomorphism

\mathcal{F}[n]\simeq j_{!}j^{-1}\mathcal{F}[n].

(7.43)

From this the perversity of
\mathcal{F}[n]
follows
as in the proof of
Theorem
2.4
(ii).
∎

Let
\widetilde{W}\subset\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}
be an open
subset of
\overline{X}^{{\rm an}}
such that

\mathcal{W}=\overline{S}^{{\rm an}}\cap\widetilde{W}
and set

W:=\widetilde{W}\cap X^{{\rm an}}\subset X^{{\rm an}}
.
Then for the restriction
\nu|_{\mathcal{V}}:\mathcal{V}\longrightarrow\widetilde{W}
of
\nu
and the morphism

\nu|_{\mathcal{V}}\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{C}}:\mathcal{V}\times\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow\widetilde{W}\times\mathbb{C}
associated to it,
we set

\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}:={\rm R}(\nu|_{\mathcal{V}}\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{C}})_{*}\mathcal{F}\simeq(\nu|_{\mathcal{V}}\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{C}})_{*}\mathcal{F}\quad\in{\bf D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\widetilde{W}\times\mathbb{C})

(7.44)

and let

\mathcal{G}:=\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}|_{W\times\mathbb{C}}\quad\in{\bf D}^{\mathrm{b}}(W\times\mathbb{C})

(7.45)

be the restriction of
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
to the open
subset
W\times\mathbb{C}
of
X^{{\rm an}}\times\mathbb{C}
.

Lemma 7.11
.

The object
\mathcal{G}[n]\in{\bf D}^{\mathrm{b}}(W\times\mathbb{C})
is
a perverse sheaf on
W\times\mathbb{C}
.

Proof.

As
\nu|_{\mathcal{V}}\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{C}}
is a finite map,
the constructibility of
\mathcal{G}[n]
is clear.
By the construction of
\mathcal{G}
,
it is also clear that the
restriction of
\mathcal{G}[n]
to the open
subset
(W\setminus H^{{\rm an}})\times\mathbb{C}
of
W\times\mathbb{C}

is perverse.
Let
j_{0}:(W\setminus H^{{\rm an}})\times\mathbb{C}\hookrightarrow W\times\mathbb{C}
be the inclusion map.
Then there exists an isomorphism

\mathcal{G}[n]\simeq j_{0!}j_{0}^{-1}\mathcal{G}[n].

(7.46)

From this the perversity of
\mathcal{G}[n]
follows
as in the proof of
Theorem
2.4
(ii).
∎

By Lemma
7.11
the micro-support
{\rm SS}(\mathcal{G}[n])
of
the perverse sheaf
\mathcal{G}[n]
is a homogeneous complex Lagrangian
analytic subset of
T^{*}(W\times\mathbb{C})
.
Then as in Section
5
,
by forgetting the homogeneity of
{\rm SS}(\mathcal{G}[n])

we define the following subsets:

{\rm SS}^{{\rm E},\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{G}[n])\subset(T^{*}W)\times\mathbb{C},\qquad{\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{G}[n])\subset T^{*}W.

(7.47)

Similarly we can show that

\Lambda:={\rm SS}_{{\rm irr}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{G}[n])
is a
(not necessarily
homogeneous) complex Lagrangian analytic
subset of
T^{*}W
. We call it
the irregular micro-support of
\mathcal{G}[n]
. Moreover,
we obtain a Lagrangian cycle
{\rm CC}_{{\rm irr}}(\mathcal{M})

supported on
\Lambda\subset T^{*}W
and call it the
irregular characteristic
cycle of
\mathcal{M}
(for the case
N=1
see
Kudomi-Takeuchi
[
39
]
). By the isomorphism

\mathcal{V}\setminus D^{{\rm an}}\simeq\mathcal{W}\setminus\partial S

we regard
f_{i}
(
1\leq i\leq k
) as (possibly
multi-valued) holomorphic functions on the open subset
\mathcal{W}_{0}:=\mathcal{W}\setminus\partial S\subset S^{{\rm an}}
of
S^{{\rm an}}

and set

\Lambda_{0}:=\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}\ \{(x,df_{i}(x))\ |\ x\in\mathcal{W}_{0}\}\quad\subset T^{*}\mathcal{W}_{0}\subset T^{*}S^{{\rm an}},

(7.48)

where in the right hand side we take the union
considering all the possible branches of
the (possibly multi-valued) functions
f_{i}
.
Set
W_{0}:=W\setminus H^{{\rm an}}
and let

T^{*}W_{0}\overset{\varpi_{0}}{\longleftarrow}\mathcal{W}_{0}\times_{W_{0}}T^{*}W_{0}\overset{\rho_{0}}{\longrightarrow}T^{*}\mathcal{W}_{0}

(7.49)

be the natural morphisms associated to
the closed embedding

\mathcal{W}_{0}\hookrightarrow W_{0}
. Then it is easy to see
that for the open subset
T^{*}W_{0}\subset T^{*}W
of

T^{*}W
we have

\Lambda\cap(T^{*}W_{0})=\varpi_{0}\rho_{0}^{-1}\Lambda_{0}.

(7.50)

Remark 7.12
.

Although the pull-backs of
f_{i}
(
1\leq i\leq k
)
by a ramification
\rho:Z^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}
of
\mathcal{V}
along the normal crossing divisor

D^{{\rm an}}\subset\mathcal{V}
form
a good set of irregular values in the sense of

[
48
, Chapter 5]
, even after shrinking
\mathcal{V}

the complex Lagrangian analytic subset

\Lambda_{0}\subset T^{*}\mathcal{W}_{0}
may be singular.

From now on, assume also that there exists a non-empty
Zariski open subset
\Omega\subset Y
of
Y=\mathbb{C}^{N}
and

R>0
such that for the open subset
A(R):=\{w\in Y^{{\rm an}}=\mathbb{C}^{N}\ |\ ||w||>R\}
of
Y^{{\rm an}}

the restriction
q^{-1}(\Omega\cap A(R))\cap\Lambda\longrightarrow\Omega\cap A(R)
of the projection

q:X\times Y\longrightarrow Y
is
an unramified finite covering and any connected component
of the open subset
q^{-1}(\Omega\cap A(R))\cap\Lambda\subset\Lambda
is a fiber bundle over a
complex manifold in
\mathcal{W}\subset\overline{S}^{{\rm an}}
contained
in
\mathcal{W}\setminus\partial S\subset S^{{\rm an}}
or

\partial S\cap X^{{\rm an}}
(see Lemma
6.3
).
Fix a sufficiently large such
R>0
and let

V\subset\Omega\cap A(R)
be
a contractible open subset of
\Omega\cap A(R)
.
Then for the decomposition

q^{-1}(V)\cap\Lambda=\Lambda_{V,1}\sqcup\Lambda_{V,2}\sqcup\cdots\cdots\sqcup\Lambda_{V,d}

(7.51)

of
q^{-1}(V)\cap\Lambda
into its connected components

\Lambda_{V,i}\subset\Lambda

(
1\leq i\leq d
)
the morphism
q|_{\Lambda}
induces an isomorphism

\Lambda_{V,i}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}V
for any
1\leq i\leq d
.
In this situation, as in Section
6
,
by
\Lambda
and
\mathcal{G}[n]\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{b}}(W\times\mathbb{C})

we define holomorphic functions
g_{i}:\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}

(
1\leq i\leq d
) and positive integers
m(i)\geq 1

(
1\leq i\leq d
) to obtain the following result.
For the proof, we use also a method similar to the one used in
Kudomi-Takeuchi
[
39
]
.

Theorem 7.13
.

In the situation as above, for the standard
holonomic D-module

\mathcal{M}=\mathbf{D}i_{S*}\mathcal{N}\simeq i_{S*}\mathcal{N}\quad\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize hol}}(\mathcal{D}_{X})
on
X
there exists an isomorphism

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes\big(Sol_{\overline{Y}}^{\rm E}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}^{\wedge}})\big)\simeq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{d}\bigl(\mathbb{E}_{V^{{\rm an}}|\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}}^{{\rm Re}g_{i}}\bigr)^{\oplus m(i)}

(7.52)

of enhanced ind-sheaves on
\overline{Y}^{{\rm an}}
.

7.3
Some Auxiliary Results

Recall that by
Theorem
4.1
(vi) for an exponential D-module
\mathscr{E}_{U|X}^{f}

on
X
associated to a meromorphic function

f\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast D)
along
D\subset X
and
U=X\setminus D

we have an isomorphism in
{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}({\rm I}\mathbb{C}_{X})

Sol_{X}^{\rm E}\big(\mathscr{E}_{U|X}^{f}\big)\simeq\mathbb{E}_{U|X}^{\rm Ref}.

(7.53)

This formula for the enhanced solution complex

Sol_{X}^{\rm E}\big(\mathscr{E}_{U|X}^{f}\big)
played
a central role in the proof of the main results
in
[
9
]
. Aiming at the study of the
Fourier transforms of general holonomic D-modules,
we shall extend it to more general meromorphic
connections. Our argument below is a
higher-dimensional analogue of those
of Kashiwara-Schapira
[
30
, Section 7]

and Morando
[
50
, Section 2.1]
. Assuming
that
M
is a real analytic manifold we
recall some basic definitions and results.

Definition 7.14
.

Let
U\subset M
be an open subset and
f:U\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
a
\mathbb{C}
-valued
C^{\infty}
-function on it.
Then for a point
p\in M
we say that
f
has
polynomial growth at
p
if for a
local coordinate system
x=(x_{1},x_{2}.\ldots,x_{n})

of
M
around
p
there exists a compact
neighborhood
K
of
p
in
M
and
N>0

such that

\sup_{x\in K\setminus U}{\rm dist}(x,K\setminus U)^{N}\cdot|f(x)|<+\infty.

(7.54)

Obviously
f
has polynomial growth at any
point of
U
. We say that
f
is tempered
at
p\in M
if all its derivatives have
polynomial growth at
p
. Moreover we
say that
f
is tempered on
U
if it
is tempered at any point
p\in M
.

For an open subset
U\subset M
we denote
by
C^{\infty,t}_{M}(U)
the
\mathbb{C}
-vector
space consisting of
\mathbb{C}
-valued
C^{\infty}
-functions
on
U
that are tempered on
U
. Then for
any pair
(U_{1},U_{2})
of relatively compact
subanalytic open subsets
U_{1},U_{2}\subset M

we have an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow C^{\infty,t}_{M}(U_{1}\cup U_{2})\longrightarrow C^{\infty,t}_{M}(U_{1})\oplus C^{\infty,t}_{M}(U_{2})\longrightarrow C^{\infty,t}_{M}(U_{1}\cap U_{2})\longrightarrow 0

(7.55)

and hence we get a sheaf
C^{\infty,t}_{M}
on the
subanalytic site of
M
in the sense of

[
29
]
. Note that this important result
is an immediate consequence of the following
Lojasiewicz’s inequality (see Bierstone-Milman
[
3
]
and
Lojasiewicz
[
41
]
for the details).

Proposition 7.15

(Lojasiewicz’s inequality)
.

Let
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}_{x}
be an open subset of

\mathbb{R}^{n}_{x}
and
Z_{1},Z_{2}\subset U
its closed subanalytic
subsets. Then for any point

p\in Z_{1}\cap Z_{2}
there exist an open neighborhood

V\subset U
of
p
in
U
and positive real numbers

C,r>0
such that we have

{\rm dist}(x,Z_{1})+{\rm dist}(x,Z_{2})\geq C\cdot{\rm dist}(x,Z_{1}\cap Z_{2})^{r}

(7.56)

for any
x\in V
.

Following
[
50
, Definition 1.1.4]
, for
a real analytic curve
\gamma(t):[0,\varepsilon)\longrightarrow M
(
\varepsilon>0
) on
M

(defined on a neighborhood of
0\in\mathbb{R}
)
we call the subset
\Gamma:=\gamma((0,\varepsilon))\subset M
of its image a semi-analytic arc
with an endpoint
\gamma(0)\in\overline{\Gamma}
.
In what follows, let
X
be a complex manifold and
consider
\mathbb{C}
-valued
C^{\infty}
-functions on
its underlying real analytic manifold

X_{\mathbb{R}}
. Then we have the following
higher-dimensional analogue of
Kashiwara-Schapira
[
30
, Lemma 7.2]

and Morando
[
50
, Proposition 2.1.1]
.

Proposition 7.16
.

Let
X
be a compact complex manifold,

D\subset X
a normal crossing divisor on it,
and
f\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast D)
a meromorphic function
along
D
. Set
U:=X\setminus D
.
Assume that
f
does not have any point of
indeterminacy on the whole
X
and has a pole
along each irreducible component of
D
.
Then for any relatively compact subanalytic
open subset
W\subset U=X\setminus D

the following conditions are equivalent.

(i)

The function

\rm Ref|_{W}:W\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
is
bounded from above.

(ii)

The
\mathbb{C}
-valued

C^{\infty}
-function

\exp(f)|_{W}:W\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
is
is tempered on
W\subset X_{\mathbb{R}}
.

Proof.

Since
f
has no point of
indeterminacy on the whole
X
, it defines
a holomorphic map from
X
to
{\mathbb{P}}={\mathbb{P}}^{1}
.
We denote it also by
f
.
This non-constant map
f:X\longrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}
being
proper and open, we obtain a
relatively compact subanalytic open
subset
f(W)\subset{\mathbb{P}}
of
{\mathbb{P}}

such that
f(W)\subset{\mathbb{P}}\setminus\{\infty\}=\mathbb{C}
. Since the proof of
(i)
\Longrightarrow
(ii) is trivial,
we shall prove only (ii)
\Longrightarrow
(i).
We prove it by showing a contradiction.
Suppose that the function

\rm Ref|_{W}:W\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
is not
bounded from above. Then there exists a
sequence
p_{k}\in W
(
k=1,2,3,\ldots
)
such that

\lim_{k\to+\infty}{\rm Re}f(p_{k})=+\infty.

(7.57)

By taking a subsequence of it, we may assume also that
it converges to a point
p\in X
. Then by the
condition
f\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast D)
,
we have
p\in D\cap\overline{W}
. By the
curve selection lemma, there exists a
real analytic curve
\gamma(t):[0,\varepsilon)\longrightarrow\overline{W}
(
\varepsilon>0
)
(defined on a neighborhood of
0\in\mathbb{R}
)
such that
\gamma(0)=p\in D\cap\overline{W}
,

\gamma(t)\in W
for any
t\in(0,\varepsilon)
, and

\lim_{t\to+0}\rm Ref(\gamma(t))=+\infty.

(7.58)

Consider the semi-analytic arc

\Gamma:=\gamma((0,\varepsilon))\subset W\subset X_{\mathbb{R}}
with an endpoint

p=\gamma(0)\in D\cap\overline{W}
.
Let
z=(z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{n})
be a holomorphic
coordinate system of
X
on a neighborhood

V
of the point
p\in D
such that we have

\{p\}=\{z=0\}
and

D=\{z_{1}z_{2}\cdots z_{l}=0\},\quad f(z)=\frac{1}{z_{1}^{m_{1}}z_{2}^{m_{2}}\cdots z_{l}^{m_{l}}}

(7.59)

for some
1\leq l\leq n
and
m_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}

(
1\leq i\leq l
). Shrinking the semi-analytic
arc
\Gamma\subset W
if necessary, we may assume
that
\overline{\Gamma}\subset V\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}
.
Then we can apply Proposition
7.15

the pair
(Z_{1},Z_{2})
of the closed subanalytic
subsets
Z_{1}=\overline{\Gamma}
and

Z_{2}=\partial W
of
V\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}

(satisfying the condition
Z_{1}\cap Z_{2}=\{p\}=\{z=0\}
) to show that there exist an open
neighborhood
V_{1}\subset V
of
p
in
V
and
positive real numbers
C_{1},r_{1}>0
such that we have

||z||={\rm dist}(z,\{p\})\leq C_{1}\cdot{\rm dist}(z,\partial W)^{r_{1}}

(7.60)

for any
z\in\overline{\Gamma}\cap V_{1}\subset V\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}
. On the other hand,
by the condition
\{p\}=\{z=0\}\subset D

we have also

{\rm dist}(z,D)\leq{\rm dist}(z,\{p\})=||z||

(7.61)

for any
z\in\overline{\Gamma}\cap V_{1}
. Set

g(z):=\frac{1}{f(z)}=z_{1}^{m_{1}}z_{2}^{m_{2}}\cdots z_{l}^{m_{l}}.

(7.62)

Then we can easily show that there exist an open
neighborhood
V_{2}\subset V
of
p
in
V
and
positive real numbers
C_{2},r_{2}>0
such that we have

|g(z)|=\frac{1}{|f(z)|}\leq C_{2}\cdot{\rm dist}(z,D)^{r_{2}}

(7.63)

for any
z\in V_{2}\setminus D=V_{2}\cap U
. Now let
us set
V_{0}:=V_{1}\cap V_{2}\subset V
. Then
there exist positive real numbers
C,r>0
such that we have

|g(z)|\leq C\cdot{\rm dist}(z,\partial W)^{r}

(7.64)

for any
z\in(V_{0}\cap\overline{\Gamma})\setminus D=(V_{0}\cap\overline{\Gamma})\cap U
.
Shrinking the semi-analytic
arc
\Gamma
once again, we may assume
that
\Gamma\subset V_{0}\cap W
. Note that the
image
f(\Gamma)\subset{\mathbb{P}}\setminus\{\infty\}=\mathbb{C}^{n}
of
\Gamma

by
f:X\longrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}
is
a semi-analytic arc in
{\mathbb{P}}

with an endpoint
\infty\in{\mathbb{P}}
. Indeed, for the real analytic curve

(f\circ\gamma)(t):[0,\varepsilon)\longrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}
(
\varepsilon>0
)
we have
(f\circ\gamma)(0)=\infty\in{\mathbb{P}}
.
It satisfies also the condition

\lim_{t\to+0}\rm Re(f\circ\gamma)(t)=+\infty.

(7.65)

Then by the proofs of
[
30
, Lemma 7.2]

and
[
50
, Proposition 2.1.2]
, we see
that for any
M>0
and
N>0
there exists
a point
\tau\in f(\Gamma)\subset\mathbb{C}

such that

|\exp(\tau)|>M|\tau|^{N}.

(7.66)

In particular, if we take increasing sequences
M_{k}>0
,

N_{k}>0
(
k=1,2,3,\ldots
)
satisfying the condition

\lim_{k\to+\infty}N_{k}=+\infty,\quad\lim_{k\to+\infty}\frac{M_{k}}{C^{N_{k}}}=+\infty,

(7.67)

there exist points
\tau_{k}\in f(\Gamma)\subset\mathbb{C}

(
k=1,2,3,\ldots
) such that

|\exp(\tau_{k})|>M_{k}|\tau_{k}|^{N_{k}}.

(7.68)

We may assume also that
\lim_{k\to+\infty}\tau_{k}=\infty\in{\mathbb{P}}
. For each
k\geq 1
we choose
a point
z_{k}\in\Gamma
such that
f(z_{k})=\tau_{k}
.
Then we obtain

|\exp(f(z_{k}))|>M_{k}|f(z_{k})|^{N_{k}}=M_{k}|g(z_{k})|^{-N_{k}}

(7.69)

for any
k\geq 1
. By the condition

\lim_{k\to+\infty}f(z_{k})=\lim_{k\to+\infty}\tau_{k}=\infty,

(7.70)

the sequence
z_{k}\in\Gamma
(
k=1,2,3,\ldots
)
satisfies the condition

\lim_{k\to+\infty}|g(z_{k})|=0.

(7.71)

Then it follows also from Proposition
7.15
that
we have

\lim_{k\to+\infty}{\rm dist}(z_{k},D)=\lim_{k\to+\infty}||z_{k}||=0.

(7.72)

Hence we get

\lim_{k\to+\infty}z_{k}=p\in D.

(7.73)

On the other hand, by (
7.64
) and
(
7.69
) we have

|\exp(f(z_{k}))|>\frac{M_{k}}{C^{N_{k}}}\cdot{\rm dist}(z_{k},\partial W)^{-rN_{k}}

(7.74)

for any
k\geq 1
. By (
7.67
) and (
7.73
)
this implies that the
\mathbb{C}
-valued

C^{\infty}
-function

\exp(f)|_{W}:W\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}

does not have polynomial growth
at
p\in D\cap\overline{W}
.
This completes the proof.
∎

As in the the proofs of
[
30
, Proposition 7.3]

and
[
50
, Lemma 3.1.1]
,
by Proposition
7.16
we obtain
the following result.

Theorem 7.17
.

In the situation of Proposition
7.16
,
for
R>0
we set

W_{R}:=\{z\in U\ |\ {\rm Re}f(z)<R\}\subset X.

(7.75)

Then we have an isomorphism

H^{0}Sol_{X}^{t}\big(\mathscr{E}_{U|X}^{f}\big)\simeq\underset{R\to+\infty}{``\varinjlim"}\ (\mathbb{C}_{X})_{W_{R}}.

(7.76)

Proposition 7.18
.

Let
D\subset X
be a normal crossing divisor in
X
and
assume that a holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{M}
has a
quasi-normal form along
D
. Let
i_{D}:D\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion map. Then for
any
j\geq 1
there exists a complex constructible
“sheaf”
F_{j}
on
D
such that we have

H^{j}Sol_{X}^{t}\big(\mathcal{M}\big)\simeq(i_{D})_{*}F_{j}.

(7.77)

Proof.

For a point
x\in D
, by our assumption
there exists a ramification map
\rho:X^{\prime}\to U

on a neighborhood
U
of
x
such that
\mathbf{D}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{M}|_{U})

has a normal form along the normal
crossing divisor
D^{\prime}:=\rho^{-1}(D\cap U)\simeq D\cap U
in
X^{\prime}
. Let

i_{D^{\prime}}:D^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}
be the inclusion map.
Then by the proof of
[
22
, Proposition 3.14]

for any
j\geq 1
there exists a complex constructible
“sheaf”
G_{j}
on
D^{\prime}
such that we have

H^{j}Sol_{X^{\prime}}^{t}\big(\mathbf{D}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{M}|_{U})\big)\simeq(i_{D^{\prime}})_{*}G_{j}.

(7.78)

Moreover, by
[
29
, Theorem 7.4.6]
and

[
24
, Propositon 4.39]
we have an isomorphism

Sol_{U}^{t}\big(\mathbf{D}\rho_{\ast}\mathbf{D}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{M}|_{U})\big)\simeq{\rm R}\rho_{*}Sol_{X^{\prime}}^{t}\big(\mathbf{D}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{M}|_{U})\big).

(7.79)

The ramification map
\rho
being finite, for any
j\geq 1

we obtain an isomorphism

H^{j}Sol_{U}^{t}\big(\mathbf{D}\rho_{\ast}\mathbf{D}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{M}|_{U})\big)\simeq(\rho\circ i_{D^{\prime}})_{*}G_{j}.

(7.80)

Since
\mathcal{M}|_{U}
is a
direct summand of
\mathbf{D}\rho_{\ast}\mathbf{D}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{M}|_{U})
,
the same is true also for

Sol_{U}^{t}\big(\mathcal{M}|_{U}\big)
and

Sol_{U}^{t}\big(\mathbf{D}\rho_{\ast}\mathbf{D}\rho^{\ast}(\mathcal{M}|_{U})\big)
.
Hence we verified the assertion locally on

U\subset X
. We can easily check that it holds
also globally on
X
.
∎

From now on, let
\mathcal{M}
be a holonomic D-module
on
X
such that for a closed hypersurface
D\subset X
of
X
we have

(i)
\mathcal{M}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{M}(\ast D)

(ii)
\rm{sing.supp}(\mathcal{M})\subset D
.

Set
U:=X\setminus D
and let
j:U\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion map. Then

L:=\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{D}_{U}}(\mathcal{M}|_{U},\mathcal{O}_{U})

is a local system on
U
. We denote its
rank by
r\geq 0
. Let
\mathcal{R}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize hol}}(\mathcal{D}_{X})

be the regular meromorphic
connection on
X
along
D
such that we have an
isomorphism

Sol_{X}(\mathcal{R})\simeq j_{!}L

(7.81)

(see e.g.
[
21
, Theorem 5.3.8]
). We thus obtain an isomorphism

\Phi:L=\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{D}_{U}}(\mathcal{M}|_{U},\mathcal{O}_{U})\simeq\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{D}_{U}}(\mathcal{R}|_{U},\mathcal{O}_{U})

(7.82)

of local systems on
U
. Fix a point
p\in U
and
let
\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{r}\in\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{D}_{U}}(\mathcal{R}|_{U},\mathcal{O}_{U})

(resp.
\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{r}\in\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{D}_{U}}(\mathcal{M}|_{U},\mathcal{O}_{U})
) be the

\mathbb{C}
-basis of the local system
\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{D}_{U}}(\mathcal{R}|_{U},\mathcal{O}_{U})

(resp.
L=\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{D}_{U}}(\mathcal{M}|_{U},\mathcal{O}_{U})
) on
a neighborhood of
p
such that
\Phi(\psi_{i})=\phi_{i}

for any
1\leq i\leq r
. Assume that there exist
holomorphic functions
f_{1}.\ldots,f_{r}
defined on a
neighborhood of
p
such that

\psi_{i}=\exp(f_{i})\cdot\phi_{i}\quad(1\leq i\leq r).

(7.83)

As
\phi_{i}
(resp.
\psi_{i}
) (
1\leq i\leq r
)
extend to multi-valued global sections of
the local system
\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{D}_{U}}(\mathcal{R}|_{U},\mathcal{O}_{U})

(resp.
\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{D}_{U}}(\mathcal{M}|_{U},\mathcal{O}_{U})
),
the same is true also for the holomorphic functions

f_{i}
(
1\leq i\leq r
). Since we
have
\Phi(\psi_{i})=\phi_{i}
even after the extensions,
their real parts
{\rm Re}f_{i}:U\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}

are single-valued. Moreover, the isomorphism
\Phi

of sheaves being compatible with restrictions,
if the section
\phi_{i}
is continued to

\sum_{i=1}^{r}c_{i}\phi_{i}
(
c_{i}\in\mathbb{C}
) along
a closed continuous curve
\gamma(t):[0,1]\longrightarrow U
in
U
such that
\gamma(0)=\gamma(1)=p
,
the section
\psi_{i}
is continued to
\sum_{i=1}^{r}c_{i}\psi_{i}

along it. By this observation, we see that the
enhanced sheaf

\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{C}_{\{(z,t)\in X\times\mathbb{R}\ |\ z\in U,\ t+{\rm Re}f_{i}(z)\geq 0\}}

(7.84)

defined on a neighborhood of
p
can be naturally
extended to the one
F
on the whole
X
such that
there exists a surjective morphism

\pi^{-1}j_{!}L\longrightarrow F
and we have

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{U}\otimes F\simeq F
.

Theorem 7.19
.

In the situation as above,
we have an isomorphism

Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\mathbb{C}_{X}^{\rm E}\overset{+}{\otimes}F.

(7.85)

Proof.

Let
\nu:Y\longrightarrow X
be a projective
morphism of complex manifolds such that

E:=\nu^{-1}D\subset Y
is a normal crossing
divisor in
Y
, the restriction

\nu|_{Y\setminus E}:Y\setminus E\longrightarrow U=X\setminus D
of

\nu
is an isomorphism and the holonomic
D-module
\mathcal{N}:={\mathbf{D}}\nu^{\ast}\mathcal{M}

on
Y
has a quasi-normal form along
E
.
Set
V:=Y\setminus E=\nu^{-1}U
.
By Theorem
4.1
(ii) and (v),
we have

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{U}\otimes Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M})\simeq Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M})

(7.86)

and

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes Sol_{Y}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{N})\simeq Sol_{Y}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{N})\simeq\mathbf{E}\nu^{-1}Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M}).

(7.87)

Since
\nu|_{Y\setminus E}:V\longrightarrow U
is an isomorphism,
there exist also isomorphisms

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\nu_{*}Sol_{Y}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{N})\simeq\mathbf{E}\nu_{*}\Bigl(\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes\mathbf{E}\nu^{-1}Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M})\Bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{U}\otimes Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M})\simeq Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M}).

Then it suffices to calculate
Sol_{Y}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{N})

for
\mathcal{N}
having a quasi-normal
form along
E\subset Y
. Let

i:Y\times\mathbb{R}_{\infty}\longrightarrow Y\times{\mathbb{P}}

(7.88)

be the morphism of bordered spaces
obtained by composing the natural ones

Y\times\mathbb{R}_{\infty}\longrightarrow Y\times P
and
Y\times P\hookrightarrow Y\times{\mathbb{P}}
for the real projective line

P=\mathbb{R}\sqcup\{\infty\}
. Then
we have an isomorphism

Sol_{Y}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{N})\simeq i^{!}Sol^{t}_{Y\times{\mathbb{P}}}\Bigl(\mathcal{N}\boxtimes\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{C}|{\mathbb{P}}}^{\tau}\Bigr)[2]

(7.89)

for the holonomic D-module
\mathcal{N}\boxtimes\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{C}|{\mathbb{P}}}^{\tau}
on
Y\times{\mathbb{P}}

having a quasi-normal form along
the normal crossing divisor

E^{\prime}:=(E\times{\mathbb{P}})\cup(Y\times\{\infty\})\subset Y\times{\mathbb{P}}

(7.90)

in
Y\times{\mathbb{P}}
.
Note that by Theorem
4.1
(v) we have also

Sol_{Y}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{N})\simeq\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes i^{!}Sol^{t}_{Y\times{\mathbb{P}}}\Bigl(\mathcal{N}\boxtimes\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{C}|{\mathbb{P}}}^{\tau}\Bigr)[2]

(7.91)

Now let us consider the distinguished triangle

H^{0}Sol^{t}_{Y\times{\mathbb{P}}}\Bigl(\mathcal{N}\boxtimes\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{C}|{\mathbb{P}}}^{\tau}\Bigr)\longrightarrow Sol^{t}_{Y\times{\mathbb{P}}}\Bigl(\mathcal{N}\boxtimes\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{C}|{\mathbb{P}}}^{\tau}\Bigr)\longrightarrow\tau^{\geq 1}Sol^{t}_{Y\times{\mathbb{P}}}\Bigl(\mathcal{N}\boxtimes\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{C}|{\mathbb{P}}}^{\tau}\Bigr)\overset{+1}{\longrightarrow}.

(7.92)

Then by Proposition
7.18
we can easily show
the vanishing

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes i^{!}\Bigl\{\tau^{\geq 1}Sol^{t}_{Y\times{\mathbb{P}}}\Bigl(\mathcal{N}\boxtimes\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{C}|{\mathbb{P}}}^{\tau}\Bigr)\Bigr\}\simeq 0.

(7.93)

Moreover, by the proofs of Proposition
7.16

and Theorem
7.17
we obtain an isomorphism

H^{0}Sol^{t}_{Y\times{\mathbb{P}}}\Bigl(\mathcal{N}\boxtimes\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{C}|{\mathbb{P}}}^{\tau}\Bigr)\simeq\underset{R\to+\infty}{``\varinjlim"}\ \iota_{!}\bigl({\rm id}_{Y}\times{\rm Re}\bigr)^{-1}G_{R},

(7.94)

where
\iota:Y\times\mathbb{C}\hookrightarrow Y\times{\mathbb{P}}
is the inclusion map,
the morphism
{\rm id}_{Y}\times{\rm Re}:Y\times\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow Y\times\mathbb{R}
is induced by
the one
{\rm Re}:\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}

(
\tau\longmapsto{\rm Re}\tau
) and the enhanced
sheaf
G_{R}
(
R\geq 0
) on
Y
is a natural extension of
the one

\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{C}_{\{(w,t)\in Y\times\mathbb{R}\ |\ w\in V,\ t+{\rm Re}(f_{i}\circ\nu)(w)<R\}}

(7.95)

defined on a neighborhood of the point
q=\nu^{-1}(p)\in V
such that there exists an injective morphism

G_{R}\hookrightarrow\pi^{-1}\nu^{-1}(j_{!}L)
and we have

\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes G_{R}\simeq G_{R}
.
Indeed, the sheaf
\iota_{!}\bigl({\rm id}_{Y}\times{\rm Re}\bigr)^{-1}G_{R}
on
Y\times{\mathbb{P}}

is an extension of the one

\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{C}_{\{(w,\tau)\in Y\times{\mathbb{P}}\ |\ w\in V,\ \tau\in\mathbb{C},\ {\rm Re}(\tau+(f_{i}\circ\nu)(w))<R\}}

(7.96)

to the whole
Y\times{\mathbb{P}}
. Then, as in the proof of

[
9
, Lemma 9.3.1]
we obtain isomorphisms

\displaystyle Sol_{Y}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{N})

\displaystyle\simeq\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes i^{!}\Bigl\{H^{0}Sol^{t}_{Y\times{\mathbb{P}}}\Bigl(\mathcal{N}\boxtimes\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{C}|{\mathbb{P}}}^{\tau}\Bigr)\Bigr\}[2]

\displaystyle\simeq\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes\underset{R\to+\infty}{``\varinjlim"}\ i^{!}\Bigl\{\iota_{!}\bigl({\rm id}_{Y}\times{\rm Re}\bigr)^{-1}G_{R}\Bigr\}[2]

\displaystyle\simeq\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes\underset{R\to+\infty}{``\varinjlim"}\ G_{R}[1]

\displaystyle\simeq\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes\Bigl(\mathbb{C}_{Y}^{\rm E}\overset{+}{\otimes}\tilde{\nu}^{-1}F\Bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq\pi^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes\mathbf{E}\nu^{-1}\Bigl(\mathbb{C}_{X}^{\rm E}\overset{+}{\otimes}F\Bigr),

where we set
\tilde{\nu}:=\nu\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{R}}:Y\times\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow X\times\mathbb{R}
and in
the third isomorphism we used the one

G_{0}[1]\simeq\tilde{\nu}^{-1}F
in the
category
{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{Y})
. It follows that we get
the desired isomorphism

Sol_{X}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\mathbf{E}\nu_{*}Sol_{Y}^{\rm E}(\mathcal{N})\simeq\mathbb{C}_{X}^{\rm E}\overset{+}{\otimes}F.

(7.97)

This completes the proof.
∎
